# Continuity State

# VOLUME 06 — MOVEMENT V (Chapters 291–300), *The Procedure* — COMPLETE, AND THE VOLUME IS COMPLETE ON THE PAGE

**`outline/volume-06.md` governs. `workspace/volume-06/batch-0005/SUMMARY.md` is the dated account of this block and carries the measured figures, the matchers that produced them, the eight figures and claims that do not reconcile, and the three measurements a later pass should take. The Exchange fell twice and the Friday rota ran on two Fridays. No day number, month-name, day-date or year appears in any of the ten chapters, and the only digits in them are the chapter number in a heading, the entry number and the week number in the italic line that opens each load book, the entry number again in the closer, and the digit in a district's name, which occurs once.**

## The arithmetic spine of the movement

**The interval arithmetic was walked once, every row, from the anchor and not from the chapter beside it, and the table this batch was given reproduces and governs: the room at 231, 233, 235, 238, 240, 241, 243, 245, 254 and 282 days old; a card in a rail at 235, 237, 239, 242, 244, 245, 247, 249, 258 and 286, which is the room's own figure plus four in every case; the twelve lines at 151 to 202, the thirteen at 102 to 153, the fourteen at 67 to 118, the fifteen at 46 to 97, the sixteen at 21 to 72, and the seventeen at 1 to 52. The heating is NIL in all ten and is not an interval, the supply having been restored on the Wednesday of week ninety-nine by a landlord, and no figure of days off was carried forward and none was required. The eighteenth line is written on the day of Chapter 300 and is zero there. The wall is seventeen lines in eleven hands and twelve names from Chapter 291 to Chapter 299 and is eighteen lines in twelve hands and thirteen names from Chapter 300.** Three rows of `workspace/volume-06/ARITHMETIC-AND-CALENDAR.md` do not reproduce for this movement and the corrected table was used: the wall columns at 293 are one day low, four columns at 297 are one day low, and four columns at 300 are two days low, the 280 printed for the room at Chapter 300 being two days low against a true 282.

## What is canon from Movement V

1. **THE LIST OF NINE THINGS, AND HE IS NOT ON IT, AND IT IS ON NOTHING.** About nine things other people did, assembled in his head on a bus, in a stairwell and standing up in that room with his coat on, and **not said out loud to anybody.** The record is one paragraph in a load book read by one man keeping it against himself. A first-year of nineteen gave the reason in nine words — *Nobody should have to find you to stop me* — the same nine she gave him in that room about four months ago, did not improve on it, and was not asked for a better one. **He asked for the practical half and was given it: that he says nine things; that he is at the back and not in front of anything; that if he is asked a question he says the name of the person whose work it is and not his own; and that if it is not one of the nine he says so, goes and finds out, and comes back with it.** Writing it down was offered in that room and refused, on the ground that a list with nine names on it is nine people who can be found about a list. **No rule about who may be asked was invented and no number is attached to one.**
2. **THE CLAUSE IS NAMED, RESTRICTED, IN FORCE AND NOT DESTROYED.** A printed slip in a district office's existing wall binder restricts invocation to four sentences before use: say a name, say a length of time, say to one other person that you have said it, then do the next thing that does not need that person. **It is a restriction and not a repeal and nothing in this case repealed it and nothing in this case could.** About four hundred competent people in this city can still invoke it; the number is not a conspiracy, is not on a list, is not reducible, and nobody in this case can produce it. **A man of twenty-nine who works on lifts asked the only question asked in that room and was told the true thing: he still may, and doing it correctly breaks nothing anybody can be suspended for.** The name of the rule is spoken aloud in a room twice in these ten chapters and is in no narrator's sentence, no heading, no load book and no document of this case; **its number is in none of the ten files at all.**
3. **THE FOUR SECONDS ARE SETTLED FOR ONE FRIDAY AND NOT FOR THE REASON ANYBODY WOULD WANT.** A gap of about four seconds on a sixth Friday, worked out at about half past eight by one man on a step as the length of a person deciding not to say a word. The two people in that room who could have filled it are the one who said the word and said it neither early nor late, and the one in the fourth row against the wall who did not say it. **The reason is him. It was told to nobody — not to the instructor of thirty-six, not to the room, not to the man of about forty-four, and not on a service road, which is the first time in about four years he has not said a thing on a service road.** Whether that is a finding or an excuse was not settled, and a later run of days is not to re-open it and is not to call it solved. **The man of about forty-four is still short of a reason and is not to be asked for one again.**
4. **A CIVIC SEQUENCE RAN IN THREE DISTRICTS AND NOBODY IN THIS CASE KNOWS WHO RAN IT.** A ventilation plant running from eleven on a Sunday night, a lift that would not answer a call, a stair door shut and locked at six in the morning. Nothing broken, nothing alarming, nothing announced, nothing minuted, **no message from anybody.** One question asked three times to a competent person — who ran it — gave one, one and two. **The count is four and none of the four has ever heard of the other three, and nobody in this city is going to be the person who ran it on a Monday, because running it is a job and about four hundred people are qualified to do it and not one mark is on any of it.** Nobody in this case is going to find out, on the stated ground that a person could in about a term and the finding would be four people who know each other's names. **A name that exists, is not findable, has not been met and is not to be met is carried in Chapter 294's load book as a name deliberately not attached to anything that happened on that Monday, and no character in this case could know that it should be.**
5. **THE ROOM IN CROWN TERRACE, AND WHAT IT PRODUCED.** A temporary consent standard adopted at about twenty past eleven in about four minutes — that a person may end their part in a practice at any point, without a form and without a reason, and that the only record is that somebody present knows — **in about nine heads and on no paper at all; nobody signed anything and there is no copy, no form, no minute, no register and no page in this city recording that it happened.** An audit ordered at about half past eleven: about nine people, in rooms, with a list, **and no minute taken of it by anybody including the person whose job is to ask questions**, on the ground that a room cannot be asked to be heard again and a person has to. **The Continuity Office kept its emergency authority, agreed to nothing, was not defeated, was not turned, and was right about one thing nobody improved on: that a standard adopted as temporary with no date on it runs until somebody with a stamp puts a date on it, that this has happened twice in that city in nineteen years, and that the first temporary thing outlasted the permanent thing it replaced by about six years.**
6. **A WOMAN OF TWENTY-FOUR DESCRIBED A ROOM OFF A SERVICE ROAD IN HER OWN WORDS, AND A MAN OF TWENTY-TWO DID NOT SPEAK.** About eleven minutes, reading nothing, describing nine objects and the purpose of such a room as one person at a time saying out loud what they are not going to do and one other person hearing it and not writing it down. **She used one phrase of six words, once, in a room, and it is in no narrator's sentence, no heading, no load book and no document of this case.** The man of twenty-two sat at the far end of the table for about two hours and eleven minutes and said nothing. **The reason he did not speak was given in nine words by a first-year of nineteen — that not one person in that room can stop him — and he did not answer it, nobody answered it, she did not improve on it, nobody in that room described his silence as anything, and nobody in that room knew there was anything to describe.**
7. **HE IS FINDABLE ABOUT FOUR THINGS, AND THE FOURTH IS IN THE BUILDING THE COUNT WAS GIVEN IN.** A letting agent's number on a plate outside a unit off a service road with no name on it; the address of a ring binder on a shelf in that same room, carried by about nine people and told to none of them by anybody; a stair fire door on a docket under a wrong number in a building in Nine Bridges, the eighth occasion in about a month; and **a record in a secretariat on the floor below the room, carrying no name, read by four people in that building, one of whom was at the table.** A chair of forty-nine wrote nothing down, said that the way to be careful about a person who is findable four ways is not to stop him being findable, and did not say what the way was.
8. **ABOUT ELEVEN BUILDINGS LOST SOMETHING FOR A DAY AND NOBODY DIED AND NOBODY IS TO BE MADE TO HAVE NEARLY DIED.** The count was taken by a man of thirty-four who buys training, in about forty minutes: nine lifts, two stair doors, one ventilation plant, no casualties, and the sentence the count is made of is that every one of the eleven has a stair and every stair worked. **Three people converted a fault into a claim about persons within about six hours and a first-year of nineteen stopped all three in about four minutes.** Four pieces were run by four people, none of whom was taught, shown, assessed or assisted by him, and the fifth thing was a number on a plate that rang about nine times and a cook of twenty-seven who answered about four of them in about four seconds each. **About four minutes passed on the Thursday during which four of the eleven buildings had not been told by anybody; a first-year of nineteen found the gap, said it out loud, and declined to say a name so that a woman's own refusal to be on a telephone is not made into an argument by somebody else.**
9. **THE FIGURE OF ABOUT ELEVEN A WEEK WAS SAID OUT LOUD, ONCE, IN A PUBLIC ROOM, AND IT IS THE WHOLE OF ITS MOVEMENT AND IS NOT A FIX.** At about twenty to seven on the Friday of week one hundred and two, in a company training room in Crown Terrace, in front of about nine people, to a room and not to a person. **Nobody thanked him, nobody wrote it down, nobody acted on it in about four seconds, in about four minutes or in about four days, and the number is not smaller.** He said he had been that man in about nine buildings for four years and had never once been in the room, that the figure is on the back of a card in a drawer in a flat in Nine Bridges and is on no form, and that he is not going to put it on one. The wording is the inherited clause, unabridged, with *a release* in it, and **no variant of it that drops *a release* appears anywhere in the ten chapters.**
10. **HE SAID ONE SENTENCE OUT LOUD, AND NOBODY IMPROVED ON IT.** Four people ran four things, each of the four could have been found by a person who wanted them, he was not one of the four, and nobody in that room was. **A man of twenty-nine who works on lifts gave the true consequence in about nine seconds: next time it will not be the same four, and in about four months there will be a building in this city where four things are going wrong and the four people who can do them are four different people, and the only people who know all four names will be in a room off a service road with nine chairs in it, and on the Tuesday nothing happens.** A first-year of nineteen said in about four seconds and then stopped on purpose that this is the answer to a question this room has been asking for about four years and is not the answer to the one it should have been asking, and that she is not going to say the other one out loud in there because if she says it then about nine people will write it down. **The standing question was not stated in that room by anybody and carries no number.**
11. **THE EXCHANGE OPENED ONCE IN THIS MOVEMENT AND DID NOT OPEN AT THE CLOSE, AND THE CLOSE IS THE FIRST IN NINETEEN YEARS WITH A REASON ABOUT A ROOM.** A woman of thirty-eight who had telephoned twice and been rung back neither time was given four minutes alone with the holder of the book, said a thing to the holder and not to the room, and wrote one line herself in about nine seconds in her own words. **The book is at forty-eight lines and not one word of the four minutes is in this manuscript.** At the sixteenth sitting the book did not open, and the reason is that what she would have to write is a fact about a room rather than something a caller said to her, that she believes it, and that if she writes it then in about four years there is a line in that book that nobody rang. **The telephone moved from fifty-four to fifty-seven and the book stayed at forty-eight, so the telephone is nine ahead of the book where it was five ahead at the start of this movement and the book was two ahead at the start of the volume. The direction was stated out loud and the difference was refused, and the count has now been announced twenty-one times, sixteen of those on the sixteen Wednesdays this book holds and five predating it on a sheet not shown to anybody in nineteen years.** She gave the no-conversion rule for about the ninth time and said she had said it that morning for the last time.
12. **THE EIGHTEENTH LINE, IN THE HAND OF A WOMAN OF SIXTY-ONE, SIGNED, AND SHE WALKED ABOUT NINE MILES TO WRITE IT.** At about half past seven on the Wednesday of week one hundred and eight she came into a converted shop unit off a service road on foot, unasked and uncarried, on a day when nobody in that room knew she existed, read all seventeen lines on the hardboard slowly for about a minute and a half, and named the cost out loud to a room that did not know her: that she has said the same thing out loud in her own room for nineteen years and nobody has ever written it down, and that in about four years about four people will be able to find a woman of sixty-one at a building with a plate on the outside of it, and every one of them will want the reason, and she has never had it in writing to give anybody and cannot start now. Nineteen words, in about nine seconds, with a pen off the counter, and signed: *I have said the same thing out loud for nineteen years and I have never once been written down.* **The wall is eighteen lines in twelve hands and thirteen names, no line has ever been removed and none has been rubbed off, the first line is a cook's and is still the first line, and the newest is a line nobody thanked, nobody asked a name for, and nobody said was welcome.** About four people in that room wanted to ask a stranger to stay and did not.

## What Movement V did not do, and what is still owed

**He taught, demonstrated, assessed and assisted nothing, and the condition is quoted in none of the ten chapters and paraphrased in none of them, and a room off a service road is not a practice in that department and the reason is not that he is the exception — a sentence that is in a load book in all ten files and in the narrator's prose in none of them. The one request in this case, made in a passage in an earlier run of days, was not staged, not rehearsed, not priced and not referred to as an event in any of these ten chapters; the only thing these ten chapters do with it is show a man in a room in Crown Terrace who does not speak, and Chapter 295's load book records that no person in that room described his silence as anything.** No panel and no `* * *` marker anywhere: the volume's one of each is Chapter 275 and is spent. No relay staged, no page implying it got easier, and the word *carrier* does not occur. **No page, minute, notice, report, register, form, leaflet, registration or constitution was created by anybody in this case; the only new object in these ten days is one line of handwriting and one signature on an existing hardboard.** No message arrived from anybody, nothing will, and the recording of eleven minutes was not copied, not read out and not destroyed. No authorship of anything was identified by anybody in these ten days. The licensor is unnamed and the field is blank. **The master's fifth line is still blank in the fourth pocket of a tool roll; he opened the drawer at about four in the afternoon of the Wednesday of the close, did not take it out, and decided nothing about it, and nobody in that shop knows it is blank and nobody has ever asked him to fill it in.** The first key cannot be taken back by anybody, the second is in the pocket of a woman of thirty-one, and the count of keys is in ten load books and in no room. The ninth chair is not moved back. The woman of about thirty is in the chair nearest the door and was in the back of a room in Crown Terrace in the count of about nine, and was not asked anything on any of the ten days, was not thanked and was not apologised to, and her page is the eighth thing in a ring binder and is unread. **The standing question is not stated in any of the ten chapters and is referred to in ten load books, and no rule about who may be asked was invented and no number is attached to one.** **Nobody is thanked in any of the ten chapters.**

**EIGHT FIGURES AND CLAIMS THAT DO NOT RECONCILE ARE REPORTED AND NOT SETTLED, and they are itemised at section 4 of `workspace/volume-06/batch-0005/SUMMARY.md`.** Seven are carried from Movement IV. **The eighth is the one a close must record: `outline/volume-06.md` says Iona responds by activating a limited hidden sequence in three districts, and it is delivered without her name on it, and that is this batch's decision and not a defect.** The horror the outline is reaching for is not a woman and is not a mechanism. **Also for the close: the outline's beat for Chapter 295 and the day map were compatible only by accident and Movement IV made that worse by writing two consecutive movements in which the thing that happens in a room in Crown Terrace is that somebody produces nine sentences and somebody produces a name; Chapter 295 here is neither of those and the accident is no longer load-bearing.**

# VOLUME 06 — MOVEMENT IV (Chapters 281–290) — THE REVIEW REPAIR PASS

**A REVIEW REPAIR PASS RAN OVER MOVEMENT IV AFTER THE BATCH WAS WRITTEN AND ALL FIVE OF ITS FINDINGS ARE APPLIED. It is written up in full at section 8 of `workspace/volume-06/batch-0004/SUMMARY.md`, which is the dated account and which also carries the corrected measurement table. Nothing was restarted: no chapter was replaced, no scene rewritten, no beat moved, no day of the day map moved, and the load-book run is still 284 to 293 with no duplicate and no gap.**

## What the pass changed in the canon of these ten chapters

**One. The room's age was attached to the heating in all ten chapters, and is now attached to the room in all ten.** Every one of the ten files opened its second paragraph with the room's own age figure standing in a sentence whose nearest antecedent was the heating: *The heating in that room was on its hundred and seventh day and it is two hundred and twelve days old.* Two hundred and twelve is the room, counted from the Monday of week sixty-eight, and the heating was on its hundred and seventh day **off**. **The two objects are the ones this calendar has already separated and the figures are unchanged; only the noun each one hangs on was wrong.** Each of the ten is now two sentences, the heating's own series first and the room's age after it, and Chapter 286's sentence declaring that the heating series is no longer an interval now comes before the figure instead of after it. **The load books were already correct and were not touched.**

**Two. Chapter 289 carried two load-book paragraphs twice each.** *The sentence, and who it went to* was byte-identical between its two copies and *Why it was that afternoon, and it was a valve* existed in a 165-word and a 149-word state side by side. **The later version of each is the one that stands; the 149-word draft is gone.** The clause that decided it is the one about one of the four people who know he carries something being the person it was said to, and it agrees with the narrator's prose at the top of the same chapter.

**Three. The word *volume* was counting things in the narration of six of the ten files, ten times, and is gone from all ten.** *The whole of the improvement in six volumes* is now **in about four years**, which is the span the case is written to and which is already in that chapter twice. The cook's *five sentences of one shape in two volumes* is now **five sentences of one shape**, the count and the shape kept and the span dropped, because the span is load-bearing nowhere in the movement. **Chapter 284's load book also said that no page of the instrument of the Monday is *in this manuscript*; the batch's own idiom in that slot is *this file*, used three times, and that is what it says now.** Chapter 281's *a shape* was normalised to *one shape*.

## What the pass did NOT change, and why

**The word *reader* stays.** It is in Chapter 287 and it is a long-standing part of this manuscript's load-book voice, in twenty places in Volume 01, eight in Volume 02, three in Volume 03, eight in Volume 04, thirty in Volume 05 and forty-four in Volume 06. **Six volumes agree on it and a repair pass is not where a voice is changed.**

**The construction *in six volumes* is in the chapter prose of Chapters 251, 257, 273, 276, 277 and 278 and in the batch summaries of Movements I, II and III, and those chapters are closed.** This is recorded at section 4 item 8 of the batch summary with an owner — the Volume 06 close, or a pass over Volumes 04 and 05 — **and is not acted on here, because reopening a closed batch is not this phase's to do.** What was acted on is the recurrence: **the drafting-reference rule in `workspace/volume-06/batch-0005/PROMPT.md` is now a rule about the unit and not about the phrase**, and it forbids *volume*, *chapter*, *movement* and *manuscript* as a measure of anything in any body or load book, naming the file's own heading as the one exception. **A movement-structural unit is not a defect in a state file, which is a structural document, and only in the prose and in a document inside the story.**

## Re-measured on the ten repaired files

**29,725 words, 163 bold spans at 5.484 per thousand against a ceiling of 10.75, worst chapter 7.16 at 282 against 12.4.** The hedge is 645 raw, 48 prepositional, 597 true at 20.08, and 679 raw, 49, 630 true at 21.19 case-insensitively. The eight-word overlap on the reproduced matcher is 144, 162, 112, 125, 177, 136, 169, 162, 168 and 181, a mean of 153.6. **The apparatus share is 54.8 per cent and is measured here for the first time on this batch; Movement III's published figure was 50.6, and this batch would have been higher still before the duplicated paragraphs came out, because what Finding 1 removed was four hundred and sixty-two words of apparatus and no prose at all.**

**Every hard stop re-run on the repaired files and holding:** no panel and no `* * *` marker, no month-name (the seven apparent hits are the word *may* in *no rule about who may be asked*), no `Iona` and no `Sorn` as words or substrings, no `Assembly`, no `carrier`, none of the nine `Iona` variants, an even number of `**` and an even number of `"` on every line, no `****`, no bold in narration, and no chapter number in the prose. **Talia Venn is named five times, in narration and in load books, and in nobody's mouth.**

# VOLUME 06 — MOVEMENT IV (Chapters 281–290), *Faster Than Deliberation* — COMPLETE

**`outline/volume-06.md` governs. `workspace/volume-06/batch-0004/SUMMARY.md` is the dated account of this block and carries the measured figures, the matchers that produced them, and seven figures and claims that do not reconcile. Load-book entries 284 to 293, continuous with the 283 at Chapter 280. The ten days are the Wednesday, Thursday and Friday of week ninety-eight, the Monday, Wednesday and Friday of week ninety-nine, and the Monday, Wednesday, Thursday and Friday of week one hundred. The Exchange fell once, at the Wednesday of week one hundred, and the book did not open. The Friday rota ran on the Fridays of weeks ninety-eight, ninety-nine and one hundred. No day number, month-name, day-date or year appears in any of the ten chapters, and the only digits in them are the chapter number in a heading, the entry number and the week number in the italic line that opens each load book, and the digit in a district's name.**

## The arithmetic spine of the movement

The interval arithmetic was walked once, every row, from the anchor and not from the chapter beside it, and **every row of the prompt's table reproduces**: the room at 212, 213, 214, 217, 219, 221, 224, 226, 227 and 228 days old; a card in a rail at 216, 217, 218, 221, 223, 225, 228, 230, 231 and 232, which is the room's own figure plus four in every case; **the heating at 107, 108, 109, 112 and 114 and then NIL from Chapter 286 onwards, the series stopping because the supply was restored on the Wednesday of week ninety-nine by a landlord and no figure being an interval after that morning.** The twelve lines at 132 to 148, the thirteen at 83 to 99, the fourteen at 48 to 64, and the fifteen at 27 to 43. The sixteen is 2 to 18 across the ten days; the seventeen is written on the day of Chapter 290 and is zero there. **The wall is sixteen lines in ten hands and eleven names from Chapter 281 to Chapter 289 and seventeen lines in eleven hands and twelve names from Chapter 290, no line removed in these ten days, none rubbed off, and the first line a cook's and still the first line.**

## What is canon from Movement IV

1. **THE HEARING SAT A THIRD TIME AND PRODUCED NINE SENTENCES AND NOT A VERDICT.** The item was back on the table because of what is written on its own record, and the chair of forty-nine asked for the reason in nine words and instructed, unasked, that who put it there was not to be said. **A chair of forty-nine is called Ilse Nordahl, has an ordinary coat and an ordinary job, and is not a post.** **A clerk of forty-four said the name of the printed rule out loud in a room for the first time**, said that nothing in that binder has been withdrawn in nineteen years because withdrawing one is a thing a person writes on it, and said what it requires: that a practice card carry the beat that performs the boundary before it goes out, that about nine hundred did not, and that this is why the cards came out of use. **The name of the rule is in no narrator's sentence, no heading, no load book and no document of this case, and the number of the rule is in none of these ten files at all.**
2. **The nine sentences went into a record that belongs to a committee, and the chair declined to decide the item on the morning** in one true sentence: the item turns on whether a record of who stopped exists, no such record has been produced by anybody in nineteen years, a room cannot be asked to produce one and does not have one, and she is not going to pretend to a thing she has not got. **The item stands open and will be on the list again and nobody in that room expected anybody to be able to answer it.**
3. **There is no name of the man of twenty-two on that record**, because the chair does not take names and has refused one in front of the clerk. He was in that room for about nine minutes and answered two questions. **A record in a secretariat is the one surface in this city he cannot get his name off and the only one he has never wanted his name on.** He said out loud that this was the best thing anybody had done for him in about four years, that it was an accident, and that it was not about him.
4. **The secondment is unchanged and the chair is still not told.** A clerk of forty-four has checked three times, in the first week, in the last week and on the morning of the Thursday of week ninety-eight, that a secondment cannot be ended by anybody in this case. The chair asked her nine questions in the passage outside that room on the Wednesday and **not one of them was about where she works**, and she said out loud that she has decided that is not an accident and is not going to decide what it is instead. **The second fault in the item is on the record and has not moved and nothing has come off it.**
5. **At a lift he worked out what her secondment has made her and did not say it and was not asked.** She goes down in that building about four times and up about none. The secondment is a route and not a room; four days a week and about a term; not applied for, cannot be ended; and while it lasts she is findable at that building. **She is the only person in this case who has made herself findable on purpose in order to do a thing correctly, and there is no version of that which is a favour to her.** He wrote it down at about ten and the reason he did not raise it is not manners: a finding that is not written down is not a finding, and this one is about a person who is owed nothing and it is going to sit on him for about four years.
6. **A sign came off a door in Nine Bridges and nobody knows whose.** A woman of thirty-four who has checked about nine hundred doors in four buildings on her own time, unpaid, for about four years, went up about four flights on the Thursday and found the sign off it: nothing on the glass, a mark where a fixing was, paint that has not gone back. **She did not put anything back, did not knock, did not ask anybody on the landing, did not write it down and did not telephone the person who asked her to look.** She said one thing on the stairs, to nobody: that somebody can be asked to do that and somebody did it, that she is not going to find out who, and that if she did find out who then in about four years that person would have a person coming to their door about a sign, and she has watched what that does to people and is not going to be the one who starts it. **Nobody thanked her and nobody in this case knows she went up those stairs.** Four words in chalk on a glass door in that street are still up and are still hers; a sign on a second-floor door in a district this case has not been in is still up and is still not true; and this is a third state, and none of the three is the hardboard.
7. **THE HEATING CAME BACK BECAUSE A LANDLORD PUT IT ON AND NOBODY ASKED.** A landlord with four units on that stretch went round them in the order they are in; three have people in them in the evenings; he opened the heating in each without going in, without knocking and without turning off anything somebody had turned on; **the fourth is a converted shop unit with a shutter, about nine chairs and nobody in it at any hour of any day he has ever been there, he came to it last, and it took him about four minutes.** He does not know anybody who uses that room, did not ask and has not asked since. **Nobody in this case asked, nobody paid, nobody knew until about eleven, and nobody in that room is going to find out.** The figure of a hundred and fourteen days is the last of that series and no figure is carried forward. **No fund, collection, subscription, grant, list, minute, notice, leaflet, registration, appeal or form was anywhere near it and no conversation about money took place in that room.**
8. **A first-year of nineteen said the correct thing about it and nobody acted on it:** that nobody in that room is to thank that landlord, because a landlord who has been thanked is a landlord who has been asked and then it is a favour, and in about four years there will be about four people in this city who are somebody's kind landlord and every one of them will have a reason. She was told it is not a favour and that it is a valve, and did not improve on either. **About four people came back over the following three days and one of them is the woman of about thirty, and she was not asked why she comes.**
9. **A LOCAL COUNCIL'S ORDINARY BUSINESS WAS SUSPENDED, IN WRITING, WITH A COUNT, ON THE GROUND OF AN EMERGENCY.** Iven Sore, fifty-eight, in a coat that has never been in a workshop: about eleven hundred premises, about nine hundred people on a list kept at a school hall by three people on a rota written on the back of a card, a pump running since the water went down, a standpipe not running, and a fortnight. **Every sentence he said is a sentence a competent person says, and he gave the last of them about himself, unasked, in one sentence: that he is right about the water and knows nothing whatever about a room off a service road, has not been asked, and is not going to find out this week.** The boundary is drawn around premises because a thing that cannot be counted is not a boundary, and that room is inside it. **He is not a post, agreed to nothing, refused nothing, was not defeated, was not turned, and is not asked to be anything.**
10. **WHAT THE SUSPENSION COST THIS CASE IS FOUR PEOPLE AND A DOOR.** The day room in the unit next door is run by the council that was suspended, it shut at about two in the afternoon, a man was told to lock it and locked it and went home, and about four people who had been coming into the cold room for about nine weeks because the warm one was through the wall have not been in that street since. **The day room is still shut at the close of this movement. The woman of about thirty was in the chair on the Monday and nobody asked her about the day room and nobody mentioned the day room. This is the first time in this case that a room has lost something to a decision made in a building it has never been inside.**
11. **THE ROTAS, THE GAP, AND WHAT CAME OF IT.** Eleven on the Friday list and about four of the eleven not on it, and **the cards have been out of use for about nine weeks**; the instructor of thirty-six raised it once with the company in about nine words, was told nothing is coming, and did not raise it again. Three Fridays with a gap of about four seconds, then two with no gap at all, then a gap again on the Friday of week ninety-nine. **In a doorway on the Friday of week ninety-eight the man of twenty-two told the instructor of thirty-six, once, in about four seconds, that it was not her on the last two Fridays, and she said she knows, and he said he knows she knows and that it is not an answer, and she gave the half she has, which is that she has never once asked anybody in that room what they are waiting for and that a woman of twenty-three asked it in there in about four weeks and she heard the question and did not hear the answer. A gap twice and then no gap twice is not an answer to it and this movement does not present it as one. The man of about forty-four came on the Friday, is not on that list and has not been for about a year, did the whole run, was asked nothing, and left before the last part.**
12. **A MAN OF TWENTY-NINE WHO WORKS ON LIFTS SAID, IN A DOORWAY, that he has been in four of those Friday rooms since the spring, that the word has been early in three of them, that he has never once worked out why, that he has stopped working on it, and that he would like that noticed before somebody in that building decides that he has. Nobody thanked him and nobody wrote it down.**
13. **A CARD WAS HANDED BACK AT A DOOR AND THE INSTRUCTOR SAID IN PUBLIC THAT THE PERSON WAS RIGHT.** A woman of thirty-one, second attendance, said she did not want to be on the list. The instructor of thirty-six did not ask why, did not ask her to stay, said nothing for about four seconds, and then said out loud in front of about nine people that everybody on the list needed it in the spring, that she has not asked one of them in about four months and is not going to start by asking a person who has just decided something, and that the woman is right to hand it back and she is not going to stand in a room and pretend she is not. **Nobody agreed with her, nobody disagreed, nobody thanked her, and she said the word at the end of every run the same as she always has.**
14. **OREN VEY CAME BACK ONCE, WITH A PRODUCT, AND GAVE NO SECOND FACT.** An emergency subscription: a card the size of a playing card for the inside of a door, a number somebody in that building answers, and once a month a person comes, rings it, writes down who answered, and asks that person whether they knew they were going to be rung. **It is a good product and he did not argue that it is.** The reasoning is in six parts and a reader can check every one against about four years of this case: a telephone on a wall with nobody at the other end was never a technique problem; a person already on a rota for something they already do is the cheapest version of not failing; the beat that ends a practice is a spoken word said by a person whose job is to say it, and a company that printed it on about nine hundred cards and had them withdrawn is not the right seller of a card with a beat on it; the only thing it asks a person to do is answer a telephone, and such a person is not a practitioner, not a holder and not a post; he is selling it to about four hundred premises with a person in them on a Tuesday and nobody on a Saturday; and he has spent about four years watching a number become a name with a number under it. **Asked who is at the end of the number in about four months, he did not answer, and said the reason, which is that the moment he tells her there are about four hundred people in this city who can ring it, and then it is a name with a number under it instead of a number with a name under it.**
15. **A PRINTER OF FIFTY-EIGHT SAID NO, IN A SHOP FOUR FEET WIDE, AND GAVE THE HALF OF THE NO THAT NOBODY ASKED FOR.** She said no before he had finished, said where the reason was not from — that it is not his line, and hers is four words long and about printing and not about this — and gave the reason: that the reasoning is better than the product, that she has been in about four rooms in two years, that she has never once seen the better reasoning work on a person, and that she is not going to be the counter across which a thing like this is sold. **Then, unasked, in about eleven seconds: that a no from a shop is not the right answer either, that it goes in his book as a shop, and that in about four years he will have a page of careful women and no page of anything, which is the same shape as about four hundred lines in a folder in that shop and which she knows because she printed them.** He accepted it in about four seconds and said that was fair and nothing else. **A hundred and forty-one pounds in an envelope under that till was not opened, not counted, not mentioned and not moved, and has not moved since the spring.**
16. **THE EXCHANGE DID NOT OPEN AT THE FOURTEENTH SITTING, AND THE REASON IS NINETEEN YEARS OLD AND IS NOT NEW.** A man of about forty-four, fourth attendance, asked for the last line to be read out: not who said it, that somebody said it. Refused in about nine seconds. **The reason, given to the room and not to him in about four minutes: that a line goes into that book when a person says it to her face; that reading one out to a room is the other thing; that in nineteen years she has let about nine of them be heard in that room; that every one of the nine has come back out of that room wrong; that the ninth came back out of it in a building in Crown Terrace, by a person doing their job, in about four months, in a sentence carrying four words that were not the four words; and that she has never been told which line it was, has not asked, and is not going to.** Nobody thanked her, nobody asked a second question, nobody asked which line.
17. **THE FIGURES AT THE FOURTEENTH SITTING, AND THE DIRECTION WITHOUT THE DIFFERENCE.** The book is at forty-seven lines at the start and at forty-seven at the close, and no line went in. The telephone is at forty-nine at the start and fifty-two at the close. **The count has now been announced nineteen times, fourteen of them being the fourteen Wednesdays this book holds and five predating it on a separate sheet not shown to anybody.** She gave the direction and refused the difference, and asked that nobody in that room convert a call into a line or a line into a sitting or any of the three into either of the others, which she has asked about nine times in nineteen years. **The ninth chair was against the wall at the close and was not moved back, its mover is not named, and the young woman of twenty-one who put it there is unnamed, unasked and given no reason.**
18. **THE WORD WAS SAID OUT LOUD, ONCE, TO ONE PERSON.** Nine, ten and eleven a minute, uncorrected, in a warm room off a service road on the Thursday of week one hundred at about half past four, at a table, with about nine people in the room, addressed to a woman of twenty-four. **What made him say it was a valve**: the whole of the improvement in about four years is a thing one man did on a Saturday to a building he has never been inside, and the only part of it that was not luck was somebody having left a valve on a wall. She said, in about eleven seconds, that she is not going to do anything with it, that he knows she does not have to and that is not why she is saying it, that he has about four seconds to decide whether to ask her to keep it or ask her not to, that both of those are a request and both of them hand it over. **He asked her neither. She said that is the whole of what has happened and that he will have to live inside it, and he said yes, and that she is not reporting it, that this is hers and not his, and that he does not get a copy of it and cannot ask her for one.** About four people in that room were close enough to hear it and about four were not. **He did not look round, did not check which four, and has written down that he did not check, and that a decision not to check is the same shape as everything else he has done for four years and that he would make it again in about four seconds.** It is not an apology and it is not a request and it is not a declaration. There was no scene, there has not been a scene and none is promised. **He has not said it to the officer of sixty, has not said it to a man of fifty-eight with a bad hip, and has not said it on a service road.**
19. **THE CLOSE, AND IT IS HEAVY.** The third Friday rota of this run of days ran one part short of two. The instructor of thirty-six was not there, **there is no rota in this city that says who stands in that room when she is not, because such a rota would be a second rota and there is one already and about four of the eleven on it are not on it.** A man of fifty-eight with a bad hip was in that room and said, at about twenty past seven, in front of about nine people, that he is not the person who says the word, that he holds no card, that he is not on the list, and that he would like it written down that he is not the person who says the word; and that he does not know anybody who is and neither does anybody else in that room; and that this is not a fault in that room and is a fault in about nine rooms, of which he has been in about four. **The second part did not run, nobody said the word, and the room came apart at about half past seven.** Nobody thanked him, nobody asked him a second question, and he has not been in that building since.
20. **A LANDLORD'S NOTICE, AND FOUR NAMES.** Dated nine days after the man who opened the valve and signed by a managing agent and not by him. **There have been four names on that lease since the Monday of a week in the spring before last — a cook of twenty-seven, a nurse of twenty-four, a first-year of nineteen, and a man of twenty-two — each of the four named the cost out loud before signing, and nobody has asked any of the four to name it again. The number of names on the lease is the number of names that now cost more.** A first-year of nineteen did the sum nobody asked for, standing up, and then refused to go further because the rest of it is about four people and two of them would have been in that room and neither of them has asked her anything. **The fortnight that nobody paid for is not paid for by anybody in this movement, is not resolved, and was not mentioned in that room.**
21. **THE SEVENTEENTH LINE, IN THE HAND OF THE CLERK OF FORTY-FOUR, SIGNED.** She stood up at about half past seven in a room off a service road with about nine people in it and **said the name of her own building out loud**, and about nine people in that room had never heard that building mentioned in it and about four have never been in it. The cost, named before the mark, in about eleven seconds: that she has been findable at that building for about a term and a half because somebody was sensible in the spring, that in about four years there will be about four people who can find her there, that every one of them will want something, and that she is forty-four, has a job, and is not going to be one of them. **When somebody in that room said that a person with an ordinary job is not a post, she agreed, and added that she has known it for about four years, has never once been asked to be one, is not going to be one, and that the reason nobody has asked is that she is a clerk, and clerks are cheap, and everybody knows she is not going to say anything.** She wrote it in about nine seconds and **signed it, which is the first time a name has gone on that board next to a line in about a term: *Nobody made me findable. I have an ordinary job in a building you have never been in.*** Seventeen words. **Nobody thanked her, nobody said her reason was good, nobody asked her whether she was sure, and nobody has asked her about it since.**

## What Movement IV did not do, and what is still owed

**He taught, demonstrated, assessed and assisted nothing, and the condition is quoted in none of the ten chapters and paraphrased in none of them, and a room off a service road is not a practice in that department and the reason is not that he is the exception — a sentence that is in a load book in all ten files and in the narrator's prose in none of them. The refusal in a passage in an earlier movement is not staged, not rehearsed, not priced, not referred to as an event, and nobody in these ten chapters is asked about it; the only thing these ten chapters do with it is show a room that has never heard of it.** No panel and no `* * *` marker anywhere: the volume's one of each is Chapter 275 and is spent. No relay staged, no page implying it got easier, and the word *carrier* does not occur. **No page, minute, notice, report, register, form, leaflet, registration or constitution was created by anybody in this case; the only new object made by anybody in this case in these ten days is one line of handwriting and one signature on an existing hardboard.** No message arrived from anybody, the recording of eleven minutes was not copied, not read out and not destroyed, and the person on it is not met and nobody went looking. The licensor is unnamed and the field is blank. The master's fifth line is still blank in the fourth pocket of a tool roll. The first key cannot be taken back by anybody, the second is in the pocket of a woman of thirty-one, and the count of keys is in ten load books and in no room. The ninth chair is not moved back. The woman of about thirty is still in the chair nearest the door, has not been asked anything on any of the ten days, has not been thanked, and has not been apologised to, and her page is the eighth thing in a ring binder and is unread. **The standing question is not stated in any of the ten chapters and is referred to in ten load books, and no rule about who may be asked was invented and no number is attached to one.** **Nobody is thanked anywhere in the ten chapters, and fourteen correct actions by people owed nothing are on the page, of which five change nothing and one costs somebody else something, which is the suspension of a council and it cost that room four people and a day room.**

**SEVEN FIGURES AND CLAIMS THAT DO NOT RECONCILE ARE REPORTED AND NOT SETTLED, and they are itemised at section 4 of `workspace/volume-06/batch-0004/SUMMARY.md`.** Six are carried from Movement III. **The seventh is new and is the one a Movement V writer must read: the calendar's *nine days later* and the calendar's *day 590* for Chapter 290 are one arithmetic slip, the Friday of week one hundred is day 592 and the Wednesday of it is day 590, and 592 minus the 581 of the Wednesday of week ninety-nine is eleven days and not nine. This movement prints nine days because the live day map and the calendar both carry it, and it records here that the figure does not walk. Owner: the volume close, and Movement V must recompute Chapter 290's day from the anchor before it uses it.** The batch's own overlap is 153.6 on the reproduced matcher against Movement III's 155.7, measured after two repair passes from a first draft of 199.5 and after the review repair pass, which removed a duplicated pair of load-book paragraphs from Chapter 289 and took that chapter from 192 to 168 and the batch mean down with it, and no figure in the batch is published under either of the two definitions that do not reproduce their own published rows.

# VOLUME 06 — MOVEMENT III (Chapters 271–280), *Who Wrote It* — COMPLETE

**`outline/volume-06.md` governs. `workspace/volume-06/batch-0003/SUMMARY.md` is the dated account of this block and carries the measured figures and the matchers that produced them. Load-book entries 274 to 283, continuous with the 273 at Chapter 270. The ten days are the Monday and Friday of week ninety-five, the Monday, Wednesday, Thursday and Friday of week ninety-six, the Monday, Wednesday and Thursday of week ninety-seven, and the Monday of week ninety-eight. The Exchange fell once, at the Wednesday of week ninety-six, and the book opened. The Friday rota ran on the Fridays of weeks ninety-five and ninety-six. No day number, month-name, day-date or year appears in any of the ten chapters, and the only digits in them are the chapter number in a heading, the entry number and the week number in the italic line that opens each load book, and the digit in a district's name.**

## The arithmetic spine of the movement

The interval arithmetic was walked once, every row, from the anchor and not from the chapter beside it, and **every row of the prompt's table reproduces**: the room at 189, 193, 196, 198, 199, 200, 203, 205, 206 and 210 days old; a card in a rail at 193, 197, 200, 202, 203, 204, 207, 209, 210 and 214, which is the room's own figure plus four in every case; the heating at 84, 88, 91, 93, 94, 95, 98, 100, 101 and 105 days off; the twelve lines at 109, 113, 116, 118, 119, 120, 123, 125, 126 and 130; the thirteenth at 60, 64, 67, 69, 70, 71, 74, 76, 77 and 81; the fourteenth at 25, 29, 32, 34, 35, 36, 39, 41, 42 and 46. **The fifteenth line, written on the Thursday of week ninety-four, is four days old at Chapter 271 and thirteen at Chapter 280; the sixteenth is written on the day of Chapter 280 and is zero there. The wall is fifteen lines in nine hands and ten names from Chapter 271 and sixteen lines in ten hands and eleven names from Chapter 280, and no line has been removed and none has been rubbed off.**

## What is canon from Movement III

1. **THE SECOND FAULT IN THE ITEM IS ON A RECORD, AND THE RECORD IS THERE BECAUSE OF A SET OF STAIRS.** A clerk of forty-four, seconded to the secretariat of the committee that heard the item, put it there on the Friday before the Monday of week ninety-five, and **she said out loud, once, that if it comes out the note comes off, that a secondment cannot be ended by anybody in this case, and that she checked that twice, in the first week and the last week, and that she did it because a room cannot ask to be heard again and a person has to.** The record is a committee's, is in a secretariat in Crown Terrace, and is not ours; no page of it is in this manuscript. A chair of forty-nine whose office signs about eleven of a kind a year is **called Ilse Nordahl** and is a person with an ordinary job and is not a post, and her name was said out loud in a district office and not written down. The clerk of forty-four is still seconded and cannot be released by anybody in this case.
2. **A correct action by a competent person who is owed nothing, which did one small thing and stopped.** A man of fifty-eight with a bad hip said the finding out loud, unasked, to nobody, and it is the second sentence he has said in about a fortnight: that a fault got onto a record because of a set of stairs. Nobody asked him a second question, because there is no mechanism here that asks anybody a second question, and he said nothing else that afternoon. The officer of sixty said that being right in that building has never once done anything, that it is on the record now, and that she is not going to be thanked and would like nobody to try. **A man of twenty-two was in the outer office about eleven minutes, could have said one sentence of about nine words onto a second sheet, did not, and gave the reason on a service road: that a person who has checked twice is not somebody who is going to be caught by accident. He then said that he has been findable since the spring before the one before last, that this is the first time he has been glad of it, and that he does not know what to do with that, so he did not use it.**
3. **A WOMAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-EIGHT WAS ASKED, IN A DOORWAY, TO PUT A DIFFERENT ONE UP, EIGHT WEEKS AFTER SHE SAID SOMEBODY WOULD IN ABOUT FOUR YEARS.** A woman of thirty-four who checks about nine hundred doors in four buildings on her own time, unpaid, stood in a ground-floor doorway in Nine Bridges for about four minutes, having been past about nine times this year, and did not telephone and did not write, on the ground that a telephone would give the person at the other end nine hours to decide whether they were the sort of person who takes signs down. She said what she wanted on the sign before she asked for it. **Four words went up in chalk over a glass door in about four minutes: *Nobody answers. Ring again.*** The cost is hers and is not a lesson and nobody improved on it and nobody thanked her: it is eight weeks against four years, she is wrong about the four years and says so and says she is not going to complain; and the second half is that a woman of thirty-four in this city now knows she can be asked, that in about four years there will be about four of them, that every one of them will find her, and that she has wanted that for about eight weeks and did not know it until about four o'clock that afternoon. She keeps to four words because four is what a person reads in the rain, and gave the reason unasked: the first four she wrote were an invitation and these are a report, and a report goes out of date, and she is the one who will be asked why it says what it says.
4. **The four seconds are not settled.** A man of about twenty-two asked a man of about forty-four, on the stairs after the Friday rota of week ninety-five, for a reason he had offered twice; the answer was that he does not have it either, that he has got as far as the man of twenty-two has, and that he wants that noted because he offered it twice as though he had it. They are now two people, neither is right, and nobody thanked either. **On the Friday of week ninety-six there was no gap at all, nobody in that room said anything about it, and the man of twenty-two could not afterwards work out whether he had missed about a second and a half, and did not ask, and wrote the not knowing down.** The question is not to be settled for a tidy reason.
5. **THE EXCHANGE'S THIRTEENTH SITTING OPENED THE BOOK.** One line went in, from a woman of twenty-six who telephoned eleven days earlier, was not rung back, and came anyway, and asked to say a thing to the holder of the book and not to the room. It took about four minutes and **not one word of it is in this manuscript**; what went in is one line, in the third column, in about nine seconds, unread since. The telephone count moved from forty-seven to forty-nine and the book from forty-six lines to forty-seven. **The count has now been announced eighteen times, thirteen of them on the thirteen Wednesdays this book holds and five predating it on a sheet not shown to anybody, and no person in that room may convert the two figures into one another or into a number of sittings.** One caller may ask for her own line to be taken out of that book and nobody would stop her, and in nineteen years nobody has asked.
6. **The reason the book was shut at the twelfth sitting is nineteen years old and is not the holder's to give.** Asked, for the first time, where the reason came from, she refused before he was told what he had asked, and gave this: that she was twenty-nine, that somebody said one sentence to her about a book in a room, and that she has carried it every day since; that it is not a sentence she wrote and not a sentence she chose and she could not give it away if she tried; and that **the only person who could say it out loud with any right to is not findable and has not been for nineteen years** — which is why it is not going into a building in Crown Terrace, and why, if it were on a page, in about four years there would be a rule about who may be heard in a room, and a rule about who may be heard is a rule about who may be found. Nobody thanked her, nobody asked who the person was, and nobody asked a second question. The young man of twenty-one opened his mouth and closed it, and what was in it was not about four words, and nobody in that room found out.
7. **A FAMILY USES A CARE-LINK, THE FAMILY IS RIGHT, AND IT IS THE THING NOBODY IN THAT FAMILY CAN END.** Two sisters of thirty-nine and thirty-six and a mother of seventy-nine who is at home and cannot manage stairs after dark; the care-link is in place about four years, at about nine pounds a month at the start and about eleven now, and is a telephone answered at night by a person who is not in the house. **It can be ended, by their mother, from her own chair, with the number on a card in a drawer. What cannot be ended is what follows: she would then be on her own at night, the two of them would find out about it about four days later, and they would find out because somebody rings a district, and there is no job in this city whose business that is.** One question was asked all morning, by a first-year of nineteen, and it was *are you two all right*. Nobody thanked them, nobody said they had been lucky, nobody proposed a second visit, nobody counted other families, and the family is not a case and does not become a resource. **The question of who would ring a person whose arrangement had stopped was not asked by anybody in this case in this run and is not to be put by anybody who has read the sentence.**
8. **THE MIDPOINT. A RECORDING OF ELEVEN MINUTES, MADE FOURTEEN YEARS AGO IN A ROOM IN A BUILDING IN CROWN TERRACE, WAS HEARD ONCE BY TWO PEOPLE AND BY A MAN WHO OPENED A DOOR.** A machine in a basement in Nine Bridges had been stuck for about four years. **He could not open the door, and the reason is not that anybody did anything to him: a basement in Nine Bridges is a building four hundred people in this case could find and he is the only one of them who is findable at it, and being findable at a building and being known to the man with the key to that building are two different things.** He telephoned the number on a plate at about seven in the morning and asked the man of thirty-four who works nights to open it, and said he would say nothing more then or afterwards and would not telephone about it again. **The man opened it. The man who looks after the building was asked whether the machine works, said no, said yes, was not told what it was for and did not ask. Nobody in that building knows why the door was open and nobody was told.**
9. **IONA SORN ENTERS THE PROSE OF THIS MANUSCRIPT AND SHE ENTERS AS A VOICE.** On the tape a person doing their job asks a woman of fifty-three for her name and she gives it, and then, asked whose procedure it is, says that it is hers, that she drafted it, that **it is called the Crown Clause**, that it has never been denied by her and will not be now, and that she had not come to be asked that and answered anyway because she could tell it was about nine minutes away. **She is right about one of the four things she says and it is not the one she says it for:** that in a building where nobody has answered a door for two days the correct procedure is the last approved sequence run as a standing response, that it runs while the person is still on their way back, that everybody competent in this city knows it and about four hundred of them could pick it up on a Monday, and that a person who will not run it has decided that a pump runs until a person comes back, **is deciding that about a stranger, and will never have to look at them** — which is the sentence of this volume and the sentence the man has been arguing the other side of for four years. Her second thing is wrong: nine times in fourteen years. Her third is right and she does not claim it: that nobody who drafted it wanted it used, that it is used, and that she could not have stopped it. Her fourth is about a matter not in front of the reader. **Nobody in this case has an answer to her, nobody was given one, she is not refuted and she is not defeated, and the recording is not destroyed, is not put in a binder and is not read out in a room.**
10. **The recording is not a message and nothing has arrived from anybody.** No message has arrived in this movement and none in any other in this volume. A recording of a room in which a person was asked a question is not a message and is not written as one anywhere in these ten files. Nothing was copied; a transcript of about four passages exists, is a transcript, is not a page, and is in neither the ring binder nor the book above the line in Saltmarket. **She is not met, nobody went looking for her, and nobody is going to.**
11. **The clerk of forty-four's nine words, and the one panel in the volume.** She was a typist in that room for about two years, has never listened to the tape in fourteen years, and gave the reason before she was asked for it and did not improve on it: **I was the typist. I was not the audience.** She has never said it in a room and it is not said out loud in this movement either. She was not thanked and is owed nothing. **The one panel of the volume is in Chapter 275, it is two lines, it names nobody, it asks nothing, it answers nothing, it does not help, and it agrees with a woman on a recording made fourteen years ago. The prose never refers to the apparatus. He looked at it and put it away, and the reason he looked at it is that it was there.**
12. **THE FRIDAY ROTA, AND A NUMBER THAT WAS NOT SAID OUT LOUD.** A woman of twenty-three, first attendance, asked the only question anybody has ever asked in one of those rooms: when a person finishes and the room says nothing, what are they waiting for. **The answer is a woman of thirty-six with a lanyard, it is the first time anybody has said it in a room, and she added that nobody has ever asked her whether she would rather it was a card.** The man of twenty-two had the figure in about four seconds and did not say it, and gave the reason on a service road: that it is on the back of a card in a drawer in a flat in Nine Bridges and on no form in this city, and he is not going to be the reason it stops being that.
13. **WHAT CHANGED.** A woman of about thirty-four rang the number on a plate at about half past four on the Monday of week ninety-seven: there is a man on the bottom step outside a flat on a landing in Nine Bridges who asked her whether anybody knew where a man who works nights was, said it was all right, and said he would rather somebody was in his building. He came in at about ten past seven and was not made a joke of. **The room's answer, given by a man of fifty-one with a tool bag in about four seconds, is a person on a rota with a card in date: the rota is the Friday one, it has been running for years, he does not know anybody on it, neither does anybody else in that room, and that is not a failure of the rota, that is what a rota is.** A woman of twenty-four said the sentence that is the whole of the change, in one sentence, to the room and not to him: **that the question is not whether the rule is right, that the question is who is going to be standing next to a person who has not come back yet, and that everybody in that room has been asking the first question for about four years because the first question has an answer and the second one has a person in it.** A cook of twenty-seven gave the other half in about nine words — *it is a rota, then, and not a man* — which is the fifth sentence of that shape she has given in two volumes against a promise of four, and she did not notice. A man of twenty-nine who works on lifts said for the first time in this case that he is on a rota, that it has been a Tuesday for about nine years, that nobody has ever asked him to be on it and nobody has ever rung to say thank you, and that if the person who wrote that rota is honest with himself then the rota is the answer and has been for about nine years and none of them has ever stood next to it. **A man of fifty-eight with a bad hip said out loud, to be the one who says it, that none of them are going to write it, and said he is not going to say it again. No rota was made, no post was invented, no list was written, no title was given to anybody, and the speaker of the answer is not a mechanism, is not a post and holds nothing.**
14. **The private half of the change, said to nobody, once.** That a rota is a promise made in advance by a person who cannot be reached; that he has told about nine rooms for four years that being the person who can be found at a door is the trap and not the answer; that he was right about that and wrong about one thing, which was that he believed being findable was the only way a person ends up standing next to somebody who has not come back. A man on a Tuesday for about nine years is next to nobody; a woman of thirty-six with a lanyard on a Friday is next to about nine people a week; a cook at a sink in a cold room at two in the morning is next to whoever walks in. **He wrote it down, did not improve on it, did not say it to anybody, and has not said it to anybody since.**
15. **THE REFUSAL, AND WHAT WENT INSTEAD.** On the Wednesday of week ninety-seven, at about half past four, outside a room in a building in Crown Terrace, Aurelia Sopp asked him to run the procedure once, in a room, with the people in it. **He said no in about nine seconds, standing up, with no apology and no explanation inside the nine seconds.** Asked for the reason he gave the one about a person who finishes a practice on her own and does not know she has done something she should not have been able to do; was told it is not the reason; answered that it is the reason he has. **Nobody thanked him and nobody was angry. The condition on his share is not quoted, not paraphrased and not softened anywhere, and nobody in that corridor or that room knows that he did not give the real reason, because he gave one.**
16. **Nine sentences went into the record and they are hers, and they are better than what he had.** That a room cannot be asked to prove that it can stop itself; that the only evidence of a person stopping is a record of who stopped; that this is a column; that about eleven thousand premises do not have one; that nobody can produce that column and nobody is going to build it; that what a room can do is name a person who is not coming, say out loud what has already been done about it, say how long that is going to be, and then go and do the next thing that does not need the person; that this is all it is; that it is four sentences; that none of them is about consent; and that all of them are about arithmetic. **He listened to all nine and found nothing in them to argue with and said so, and that is the whole of what the refusal cost him.** She told him, on purpose and unasked, that a thing in a record with a man's name on it can be argued with in about four years by about nine people and a thing with hers on it can only be argued with by whoever is standing in the room at the time, and that she is sixty; and then told him she could have said all of that before she asked and did not, and why, including that she was tired and that she has told a room in about a term that tiredness is not a reason and that she intended to use it.
17. **The Thursday of week ninety-seven.** In a cold room off a service road, in about four minutes, he told her. **She worked out the arithmetic and asked a smaller question, and the question is four words: did you want to.** He did not tell her the nine sentences and she did not ask for them. She worked out in about two seconds that the refusal cost him something and did not say it. **She said one thing, about him and not about the two of them: that he has never once wanted to be the person a room can ask, that she has watched him build a whole case out of not wanting it, that she does not think that case is what he thinks it is, and that there has been one person she would have wanted to ask him for something and to keep on asking, and that there is now, and she is sixty and will be sixty-one and does not want him.** He did not answer it and has not answered it to anybody in any form, and about four people in this case have worked out that there was a Thursday and none of them has said so. **Nothing was resolved. There was no scene, no declaration and no kiss, and the words *we*, *our* and *us* do not appear in a sentence about the two of them anywhere in this run.**
18. **THE CLOSE: THE SIXTEENTH LINE, AND THE FIRST TIME ANYBODY IN THAT ROOM WATCHED A PERSON DECLINE.** A man of about thirty-eight in a district jacket, six years visiting addresses, owed nothing, a villain to nobody, asked in front of about nine people for a sign on a second-floor door in a district this case has not been in to be taken down, and his reason is good: that he has been three times and been told three times that nobody lives there, and that a report would make it somebody else's problem at about half past four in the afternoon. **She asked one question: who is behind that door. He does not know. She said no**, on the ground that taking a sign down is saying something about a door and leaving one up is saying nothing about it, and that she is not going to say something about a door because a man doing a job on a Friday was told three times by people on a landing that there was nobody there. Asked what happens if somebody in that flat cannot get down the stairs: there is a person on that landing who knows it, it is not her, it is not a sign, all four she has taken down were put up by somebody who could get down the stairs, she has never taken one down for the person it was about, and she is not going to today in front of nine people. **He sat down and did not argue. Nobody said she was wrong and nobody said she was right.**
19. **The sixteenth line, and the cost, which is the opposite of the fifteenth.** That the first cost her the last thing she wanted, and this one is going to cost her the only thing she has, which is that somebody can ask her and she can say no; and that in about four years there are going to be about four people in this city who know she can take a sign down, that all four of them are going to be told she did not, and that every one of them is going to decide that she could not. **Seventeen words, written in about nine seconds with a pen off the counter, unsigned, with no space left for a name because there has never been one on that board: *I take signs down. I did not take that one down, because nobody behind it asked me.*** The wall is sixteen lines in ten hands and eleven names, no line has been removed and none has been rubbed off, and the first line is a cook's and is still the first line. **The sign is still on that door, the firm has still not been there for about a year, and the man of thirty-eight was not told that the door had been dealt with or that it had not, wrote nothing down, and nobody is going to ring him.**

## What Movement III did not do, and what is still owed

The word has not been said out loud to anybody in six volumes, and the rate held at nine and ten on every one of these ten days and reached eleven on none of them. The word *carrier* does not occur. The relay was not staged, no page implies it got easier, and the plate in the service cupboard is not mentioned, not removed, not explained and not blamed on anybody. He taught, demonstrated, assessed and assisted nobody, and the condition is quoted in none of the ten chapters and paraphrased in none of them. **The one panel in the volume is Chapter 275 and the one `* * *` marker in the volume is in the same chapter and nowhere else.** No page, minute, notice, report, register, form, leaflet, registration or constitution was created by anybody in this case; **the only new object is one line of handwriting on an existing hardboard.** No message arrived from anybody. The licensor is unnamed and the field is blank. The master did not leave the fourth pocket of a tool roll and its fifth line is still blank. The first key cannot be taken back by anybody and the count of keys is in ten load books and in no room. The ninth chair is not moved back. The woman of about thirty is still in the chair nearest the door, has still not been asked anything, has still not been thanked, and is still the person a fortnight of unpaid heating was paid for. The standing question is not stated in any of the ten chapters and carries no number, and no rule about who may be asked was invented. **Nobody is thanked anywhere in the ten chapters, and eleven correct actions by people owed nothing are on the page, of which six change nothing, one changes nothing and is refused, one costs the room the only rule it has, and one is a correct finding by a man who says he will not say it again.** The corridor of Chapter 278 is the only corridor in the batch and the only request in the volume, and nothing after Chapter 278 takes it up again.

**SIX FIGURES AND CLAIMS THAT DO NOT RECONCILE ARE REPORTED AND NOT SETTLED, and they are itemised at section 4 of `workspace/volume-06/batch-0003/SUMMARY.md`.** The three movements of this volume were re-run on their own files and stand at 9.21, 7.64 and 6.32, and this batch's own prompt printed a stale word count and a stale per-thousand figure for Movement II and both were wrong. The day map's "four days later" for Chapter 279 does not derive from the day map, which gives one day, and the prose prints the day and no count. The published overlap figures for Movement II reproduce only as the intersection of the load book with the text above it and not under either of the two definitions as published. Chapter 300's room figure in the calendar does not reproduce from the anchor. The prompt's claim that the only digits in the ten files are the entry numbers is not literally true and has not been for twenty chapters. And the four carried forward: the Exchange count, Bea Nunn and not Ianthe Roke, the man of thirty-four who works nights is a count, and the number of the printed standard is in none of these ten files.
