# VOLUME 06 — MOVEMENT II (Chapters 261–270), *Who Can Ask* — COMPLETE

**`outline/volume-06.md` governs. `workspace/volume-06/batch-0002/SUMMARY.md` is the dated account of this block and carries the measured figures and the matchers that produced them. Load-book entries 264 to 273, continuous with the 263 at Chapter 260. The ten days are the Monday, Wednesday, Thursday and Friday of week ninety-two, the Monday, Wednesday and Friday of week ninety-three, and the Monday, Wednesday and Thursday of week ninety-four. The Exchange fell once, at the Wednesday of week ninety-two, and the book did not open. The Friday rota ran on the Fridays of weeks ninety-two and ninety-three. No day number, month-name, day-date or year appears in any of the ten chapters, and the only digits in them are the entry numbers 264 to 273.**

## The arithmetic spine of the movement

The corrected card-in-a-rail column was applied first and not as a warning: the ten load books carry 172, 174, 175, 176, 179, 181, 183, 186, 188 and 189, each the room's own figure plus four, and the series is continuous across all nine chapter breaks in this batch. The room is 168, 170, 171, 172, 175, 177, 179, 182, 184, 185 days old. The heating is on its sixty-third day at Chapter 261 and its eightieth at Chapter 270, unpaid, unresolved, and with no fund, collection, subscription, grant, list, minute, notice, report, form, leaflet, registration or appeal anywhere near it; it comes back in Chapter 285, the Wednesday of week ninety-nine, by a landlord, and the notice arrives in Chapter 290. The twelve lines are 88 to 105 days old, the thirteenth 39 to 56, the fourteenth 4 to 21, and **the fifteenth line was written on the day of Chapter 270 and is zero days old there. The wall is fifteen lines in nine hands and ten names, no line has been removed, and the first line is a cook's and is still the first line.**

## What is canon from Movement II

1. **THE HEARING WAS HELD AND IT PRODUCED ONE THING AND THE THING IS NOT A VERDICT.** One morning, a room in Crown Terrace, about nine chairs, a chair of forty-nine whose office signs about eleven of a kind a year, who asked six questions each smaller than the one before — three of the clerk of forty-four, one of the officer of sixty and two of him — and who said at about eleven twenty-five, to the room and not to any person in it, twelve words: that a room cannot ask to be heard again and a person has to. **That is the sentence the volume is built on and it was produced by a person with an ordinary job, who is not a post, and it is owed to nobody and it is on nobody. The record of the hearing is a committee's, is not ours, and no page of it exists in this manuscript or in the room off the service road.**
2. **The second fault in the item is open.** That a practice cannot show anybody may stop at any time, because the only evidence is a record of who stopped, that a record of who stopped is a column, and that about eleven thousand premises have no column. Aurelia Sopp, sixty, whose job it is to ask the questions, raised it in the room, was answered with a smaller question, and the item went ahead. She had found it a fortnight earlier, said it once, and did nothing about it, and said again that she had done nothing and was not going to. **The item is with her.**
3. **THE CLERK OF FORTY-FOUR IS STILL SECONDED AND THE CHAIR WAS STILL NOT TOLD AT THE END OF THE MORNING.** The secondment cannot be ended by anybody in this case. The man of twenty-two saw the shape of it at a lift, did not raise it, and was not asked to. **A chair of forty-nine may be named in Movement III and was not named in Movement II.**
4. **Talia Venn, twenty-four, gave about eleven minutes of evidence about a practice she has been inside of for a term and a half, including the beat in which a person knows they are finished, and did not read the sentence on the wall binder.** She said what a card is for and not what a district says about a card, and those are two sentences and one of them is hers. The strongest evidence in that room went into a folder somebody else keeps and she was thanked by nobody.
5. **The Exchange's twelfth sitting did not open the book, and the reason is the first in nineteen years that is about the room and not about a person: a hearing is a book, and a book of what a caller said cannot be written by the people who did not call.** The telephone count moved from forty-four to forty-seven and the sign changed: the book was two lines ahead four weeks earlier and the telephone is one line ahead now. She stated the direction out loud once and did not look into it, and asked that no person present convert the two figures into one another or into a number of sittings. **The count has now been announced seventeen times, twelve of them on the twelve Wednesdays this book holds and five predating it on a sheet not shown to anybody. The book is at forty-six lines and the next sitting is the Wednesday of week ninety-six, where it opens and goes to forty-seven.**
6. **She did not ring the one caller who is not coming.** A question of hers would make any answer a reply to her question rather than an account, and she would not enter the first line in nineteen years she had entered because she had asked somebody about it. He did not come.
7. **The ninth chair is against the wall and is not moved back; its mover is not named; the holder of it is a young woman of twenty-one in attendance since fifteen, unnamed, given no reason, and not asked anything.** The young man of twenty-one in attendance since seventeen keeps a count, was given the same answer for the fifth time, was not to be confirmed, and was not to be stopped.
8. **A delivery stoppage over a company requirement cost a first-year of nineteen a friendship for a term, and she named the cost at a sink, unprompted, and was not thanked.** A woman of about forty-four came into the room off the service road because the room is open, asked whether she could sit, was answered in four words, sat about forty minutes and was asked nothing. **Asha Reed is not going to be her friend for about a term, and the ground is that the person on a stoppage is findable at that door and so is the person who is her friend, and she is on about eleven hundred pieces of paper with her own name at the bottom of them by her own choice.** Nobody improved on the cost; about four people wanted to say it had not been her choice and none of them did. The stoppage was settled by the company and its drivers in about four minutes on an afternoon in the following week, without anybody in this case.
9. **The refusal of a fourth request to write down the ending of a practice is now four words: cards go in pockets.** The long form was given in about thirty words on the Friday before last, which is the Friday of week ninety and the same Friday she asked the first time, was given a second time on being asked whether it had changed, and will not be given a third. The asker said she would ask a fifth and is not asking because she thinks the refusal is wrong. **On the last run that Friday nobody waited: about four seconds, and whether that is because the cards have been out of use for about two weeks — the withdrawal was the Wednesday of week ninety, sixteen days earlier — or because the person in that chair is not the person who was in it on the Friday before last was not established and is not to be settled in this volume. It is still not established: on the Friday of week ninety-three the same woman was in the second row for a second consecutive Friday and finished a run in the same four seconds, and a man of forty-four said in a doorway that the same gap had occurred the week before and that whoever wants the reason may have it, and nobody in that room asked and the man of twenty-two did not start it.**
10. **A woman of sixty-one was asked to give evidence and said no, in ten words, and the ten words are: she keeps the book and is not in the book. Those ten words are not the sentence she has been refusing in for nineteen years and she took care of that before she said them, and one man in that room heard the difference and has never been told what the other sentence is.** Asked once, by Aurelia Sopp, who went to the room above the line in Saltmarket on the Monday morning of week ninety-three. The rest of the reason was given once and withdrawn; a page of forty-one cannot go into a system because it would work, and if it did not work she would be doing it. **She is owed nothing, was thanked by nobody, is not carried anywhere, is not put on a stage, is not described as a resource, and is not going to be asked again. This is the only time in three volumes she was asked.**
11. **The object of the volume was named as about nine hundred and stopped being a number of objects inside about four minutes.** A man of fifty-one with a tool bag said the number of doors and was not thanked. A woman of thirty-four, Petula Fenn, said the checking has to be done by a person because a number is on a wall and the list is on paper, that there is no money for it, that the list is reissued about four times a year and never corrected, and that she is the person and it is her own time. **The four minutes a door is the mean and not the best case: it is about nine where there is no lift, two of the four buildings have none, and about nine of the doors are in them, which she knows because she has done them on the stairs. She is the only named person in this movement who is not in `outline/volume-06.md`, and a later writer may not treat her as if the outline had authorised her.** A first-year of nineteen did the arithmetic out loud: nine hundred doors at four minutes is sixty hours, four issues a year is two hundred and forty hours, which is **about six weeks of a working year, every year, for one person, unpaid, unasked for and not stopping.** She has asked twice in writing; the second reply calls the list a guide. **Nobody asked to see the card. She did not produce it, on the ground that then it would be in the room.**
12. **A plate in a service cupboard on a fourth landing in Nine Bridges is not explained, is not removed and is not blamed on anybody, and what is wrong with it is that nothing is wrong with it.** Found by a man of thirty-four who works nights, who did not touch it, did not open the box behind it, did not photograph it and did not report it, and who told one person by telephone at about ten past five on a Friday morning. It carries the block's own number, four things in a box on its face and no fifth, and it was not there in the spring. **He was told it would not be written down and said it would be, and that he would rather be written down than asked twice. He was asked what the fifth thing is and was not told, and the ground is that explaining it would make him the second person in this case who has been told. He has not been back into that cupboard and has not been asked to go.**
13. **The man of thirty-four who works nights is the only person in this case who can now be found at a door, and that is the last thing he wanted, and he has wanted it not to be true for about four years.** He named the cost out loud before writing the fifteenth line and named the mechanism himself: he works nights, he knows which four hundred buildings in this city have a landing and which nine hundred doors carry a number off a list somewhere, a first-year of nineteen is on about eleven hundred pieces of paper and knows it, a man of fifty-one has a company and a tool bag, a cook is a cook, and the man of twenty-two is reachable only through a company in another district. **The fifteenth line is fourteen words, unsigned, with a space left where a name would go, and two people looked at it. Nobody thanked him, nobody said the reason was good, nobody argued, and nobody asked him whether he was sure.**
14. **The four words on the first line of the hardboard are on about four hundred lines in about four years in about nine buildings the man of fifty-one has worked in, in a different hand every time, about two hundred and forty of them on one sheet about the size of a piece of kitchen paper taped to a service corridor wall in Crown Terrace and the rest about ninety on a landing in a second building, about forty by a bin store in a third, and the remainder on about nine notices in as many more and in about four ledgers in four offices.** It was found by a man of fifty-one with a tool bag on a job, by accident, and nobody had been looking for it. **The cook of twenty-seven who wrote them was at a sink in the room off the service road while all of it was said, and nobody told her, and a first-year of nineteen gave ten words — they are hers, and four hundred lines is a bigger surface — and then gave the whole ground when asked for the rest of it, and about nine people said nothing at the same time.** She does not know and is not going to be told. He did not photograph the sheet and gave as his reason that the corridor has a camera in it and that he does not put things in buildings where he has been asked to leave a name.
15. **The oldest hand on that sheet is in the second column about nine lines down. Two people in that room had seen it before and neither said so. The man who wrote it was given an answer by a stranger in that building about four years ago, was sixty-one, and has gone quiet. A room of about forty people in Crown Terrace knows he still has nine words of his own. Nobody in this case can hand him four words, because he cannot be found, and there is no mechanism in this city for arriving at a person.** He is not named, he was given nothing, nothing has been sent to him, and nothing will be.
16. **A page of about forty-one people was read out loud once, in about nine minutes, to about nine people in a back room above a counter in Bower Street, by a clerk of fifty-three who has worked that counter for nineteen years and has been asked about four things in that time.** Her reason, in one sentence, given before she started: a page nobody has read is a page nobody can close. She said before she began that it was not going to do anything, worked out halfway down the list that it was not going to do anything, and read the other half anyway. Nobody spoke between the names. About nine of the forty-one were telephoned before whatever happened; one who was not was safe; the other thirty-one have not been heard from and will not be, and the district officer who asked about a reference put the arithmetic correctly as nine rung, one not rung and safe, and thirty-one others not rung, and the room had heard all forty-one read aloud in nine minutes. **A district officer asked whether it could be recorded properly with a reference and was refused, on the ground that it would work. The pencil box on that counter was not emptied, took no second name, was not given a reference number, was not moved onto a system, and was adopted by no district.**

## What Movement II did not do, and what is still owed

The word has not been said out loud to anybody in six volumes, and the rate held at nine and ten on every one of these ten days and reached eleven on none of them. The word *carrier* does not occur. The relay was not staged and the woman who carried it in Chapter 136 was not asked again and is not in this volume. He taught, demonstrated, assessed and assisted nobody, and the condition is quoted in none of the ten chapters and paraphrased in none of them. No panel and no `* * *` marker. No page, minute, notice, report, register, form, leaflet, registration or constitution was created by anybody in this case; **the only new object is one line of handwriting on an existing hardboard**. No message arrived from anybody. The licensor is unnamed and the field is blank. The master did not leave the fourth pocket of the tool roll. The first key cannot be taken back by anybody. The ninth chair is not moved back. The woman of about thirty is still in the chair nearest the door, has still not been asked anything, and is still the person the fortnight of unpaid heating was paid for. The standing question is not stated in any of the ten chapters and carries no number. **Nobody is thanked anywhere in the ten chapters, and eleven correct actions by people owed nothing are on the page, of which four change nothing and one costs somebody something.**

**FOUR FIGURES THAT DO NOT RECONCILE ARE REPORTED AND NOT SETTLED, and they are itemised at section 4 of `workspace/volume-06/batch-0002/SUMMARY.md`.** The Exchange count is one line ahead of the book at the close of this batch and the batch prompt says two. The person of sixty-one is Bea Nunn and the outline's beat for Ianthe Roke, thirty-one, is not paid and must not be treated as a debt. The man of thirty-four who works nights is still a count and he writes the volume's fifteenth line; the volume close owns his naming. The number of the printed standard does not appear in these ten files at all, in prose, in a heading, in a load book, or in a document inside the story.

Canon established in the bible, the series and the volume outlines, and now in Chapter prose through Chapter 160. **This file was compacted in the review repair pass of Volume 04 batch 0001: the per-chapter canon blocks for Chapters 1–150 were replaced with an archive section that points at the document that now carries each range, and the full text of every removed block is in the git history of this file. The state at the end of Chapter 150 is the `VOLUME 03 CLOSED` block below and the live state is the `VOLUME 04` block below that.**

## Core canon

- Setting: Nacre, a modern coastal city-republic. The formal public network is the Civic Weave; the common name is the Soul Network.
- Protagonist: Marek Senn, 22, third-year at the Nacre Institute of Applied Practice, night repairer at Civic Spine 7. He wants a license, a wage, and to stop being the family's problem.
- Marek's flaw is taking responsibility before being asked and hiding its cost. His need is to accept chosen interdependence without turning it into ownership.
- Primary romance: Talia Venn, resonance nurse and consent advocate. They are not partners at the opening.
- Family: Lena Senn is Marek's mother (nights at a long-stay clinic on Kestrel Row); Nell Senn is his younger sister, 16; Evan Senn is his missing father.
- Recurring allies: Rafi Pell (roommate, courier), Sera Quill (Spine 7 lead repairer), Asha Reed (Movement IV onward), Dessa Kwan (from Chapter 10).
- Central antagonist: Dr. Iona Sorn, the Conductor. Oren Vey, Iven Sore and Leo Marr represent commercial, institutional and privacy pressures before the Crown Clause's final use.

## Power rules

- A share carries observations and technique structure, not private thoughts, innate talent, or full memories.
- Consent is scoped and can be withdrawn. Use, Remember and Derive are separate permissions. Silence, proximity and a pre-signed workplace form are not agreement.
- Connections produce aftertaste and attention debt. Overload can cause physical symptoms, emotional bleed, and lasting unmooring.
- A hinge is not merely a missing boundary label. It deletes the boundary-release beat that should apply the limit and complete the practice, then installs a key-holder's cue as the learner's expected replacement; it narrows practical independence but is not universal mind control.
- The opening brace is distributed by Threadline from an unnamed upstream technique bundle. Threadline is the contracted assembler and seller, not the author of the Crown Clause.
- Marek begins at Stage 0, Latent Counterpoint. His eventual stages end with Open Weave and the final Unbinding.

### Volume 01 — *The Missing Beat*, Chapters 1–50, and what later volumes inherit from it

The rescue brace in Threadline's kit revision 4-19 has its fourth boundary-release beat deleted and replaced by the instructor's spoken cue, and it has only ever worked because everyone in the room was taught to wait for that voice. **That definition of a hinge is canon and is still the definition: a hinge is not merely a missing boundary label, it deletes the boundary-release beat that should apply the limit and complete the practice and installs a key-holder's cue as the learner's expected replacement.** The volume's question was not whether Marek could pass an exam but whether a practice can be finished by the person doing it, and the answer was delivered by other people refusing to wait: Tam Arrick telling him to stop speaking for him and writing his own recovery plan, Nkechi Osei telling him a list on a wall is not the thing she asked for, Yvette Kohl refusing the category, a records clerk drawing a ruled box headed UPSTREAM LICENSOR with nothing in it because the co-operative will not invent one.

Load-bearing and still true: **Marek never receives the Solo Seal**, and the seal line stays as written — *not completed. Fourth beat absent. Performance dependent on external cue.* The provisional Working Share is granted in Chapter 49 and never earlier, with seven headings, four named partners who may end it by word, and a fourth condition. **Tam Arrick's forearm injury, his lost kitchen wages and his slow recovery are permanent costs and are never reset. The Institute's manual is still 3-08 and nothing in any volume repairs the paperwork. The Crown Clause is never named in prose. Nell Senn does not appear and is not written about.** Threadline is the contracted assembler and seller and is not the author. The three accounts of one morning — the Institute's, the Choir of Practice's, the Quiet House's — do not match, none of them is wrong, and there is no office in this city whose job it is to reconcile them. **A rule that governs a page cannot govern a mouth** is the volume's last finding and the hand-off, and it is Rule 1 in the maintenance ledger, copied from Bea Nunn's board at the Exchange with the credit on it.

### Volume 02 — *Threadline*, Chapters 51–100, and what later volumes inherit from it

He is offered a wage, a training certificate and an address by the company that sold the kit, and has to work out which of the three he is allowed to want. **The certificate is a door for a twenty-year-old whose middle finger has not moved, and that is the volume's moral centre and it is never said out loud in those terms.** Threadline's advantage is not concealment: a standardized technique with a trained holder in it produces fewer reportable accidents, and a city that has just been frightened by a hinge wants fewer accidents. **Threadline is not defeated in this volume and must not be in any volume** — it is shown to be a conduit, made answerable on a narrow set of facts, and left standing as a legal and political power.

Load-bearing and still true: the local lab and Threadline's relevant supply records are exposed and the kits are recalled, **and the recall is a company doing the right thing for the wrong reason, and Marek is not the reason for it and does not get the credit.** Rafi Pell recovers with a counterpoint routine he wrote himself, which has no certificate and two named people who can end it, and joins a worker co-operative. **Marek keeps the job and loses the argument, and the job is the thing that makes him a key-holder on a company's rota with a supervisor who can confirm his releases, and he takes it anyway.** The fifth room stays shut, and the question of who takes the name out for the people who will sit in rooms that cost them one is answered with **nobody, and a list of the four hundred people who will be told by somebody kind that it was the price of the room.** Sera Quill's maintenance work, the cage she has taken custody of and lifted nothing from, and the printed label **KIT 4-19 — DO NOT REISSUE** with no name and no date on it. The power state at the end of the volume is Stage 2 Counterpoint with a costly relay and a permanent sensitivity, and **the demonstration in Chapter 97 fails and he is the reason.** Milestone 1 was spent in Chapter 54 and Volume 02's milestone 3 in Chapter 99 in nine words.

### Volume 03 — *The Unquiet House*, Chapters 101–150, and the volume is closed

The rota, the levy, the funded third line of a specification, the counterfeit hinge that is the same shape as the original and is the one that gets noticed, and the word that cannot be turned off. **The whole of Volume 03's per-chapter canon is not restated here. It is in the `VOLUME 03 CLOSED` block below, which is the state at the end of Chapter 150, and in `workspace/volume-03/batch-0005/VOLUME-CLOSE.md`, which is the dated hand-off and is the document Volume 04 was written against.** The four things a Volume 04 writer must carry forward and can read in those two places rather than here: **the licensor is unnamed and the field is blank and the Crown Clause is never named and nobody identifies an authorship; the seven are still seven and the four things that are not the seven are still four and the word *pattern* appears nowhere; Asha Reed's sentence has been said seven times and is never said again; and the Exchange's book is at forty-four lines with a hole in line forty-four and does not go to forty-five in this volume.**

# VOLUME 03 CLOSED — "The Unquiet House" (Chapters 101–150), and what Volume 04 inherits

**Volume 03 is CLOSED. The load-book run ends at entry 153. Volume 04 begins at Chapter 151 and its first load-book entry is 154.** The full dated hand-off is `workspace/volume-03/batch-0005/VOLUME-CLOSE.md` and it supersedes this block for anything about what is still open; this block is the load-bearing canon a later writer quotes rather than re-derives.

## What Chapters 149 and 150 added to the record, in the order it is established

1. **Ch 149, the Wednesday of week 45 — the volume's resolution, and there is still no post.** A counter on the ground floor of a building in Bower Street with about two hundred of the district's withdrawal pages on it in four stacks, uncalled for since the spring: **four fields, one of them a tick.** A date, a form, a person whose job it is to telephone, and **a box on a form instead of a post.** **Petra Lindqvist, fifty-three**, nineteen years at that counter, has been told twice this term that there is no box for it — once by a woman in the spring who did not give her name and was right, and once by a printed line at the foot of a district instruction that says no local additions. **She is not asked to break the instruction: she is asked to draw a line in pencil, write three words over it in her own hand, put nothing in it today, and take the return.** She rules it in under the fourth field, about the size of a stamp, and writes *who ended it* over it. **No reference number, nothing typed, nothing copied, nothing entered anywhere, a form and not a system, and the only box in this city for the person who removed a share.** The cost, in her own words: **a date and a time and a telephone number and a name at the bottom of it is a person who cannot be ill, and cannot be on a Friday, and cannot be on a Thursday, and I have a job on a Thursday, and if I stop doing it the form does not stop.** **The box is empty on the day it is made, and at a quarter past two she telephones the first one on the diary, because a date is not a telephone call; it took four minutes and the share came off at the request of the person it concerns, which is the only case the four fields were written for.** The person said thank you and was told not to. **Nobody thanked her and the clerk has not mentioned her since.**
2. **Ch 149 — what Ianthe Roke had to work with was nine seconds, and it is the Chapter 145 promise paid silently.** A district nurse of twenty-four rang that office on the Thursday of week forty-four, about her own reasons, as she has for four years, and **said the name of a district and nothing else.** It is in a diary and the page has not been shown. **Neither woman ever refers to it again, and if the woman of thirty-one has understood where it came from she has not written it down, and Marek Senn does not know.**
3. **Ch 149 — THE PLATE'S ELEVEN WEEKS ARE PAID, ON THE PAGE, AS A CORRECT ACTION THAT CHANGED NOTHING.** Somebody came in the week of week thirty-seventh with a fault ticket that carried no name on it, read it against the wall, **wrote 2-14 on the wall again in the building's own typeface and closed the ticket.** The batch brief had recorded this event in the load books and left it out of the prose; **Chapter 149 is where the prose takes it.** The room is still on a shelf, about four hundred consent leaflets in the rack are all printed 2-07, about forty-one people in that district are on a link nobody can end, and the only route out of that corridor is the counter.
4. **Ch 149 — the page the box is for already exists and cannot be fixed.** One of the same forms in a drawer upstairs, four fields filled in, the tick, a date and a time in a hand that is not hers, from the Tuesday of week thirty-eighth, when a care-link came off a woman of sixty-eight in four seconds and a bay went off eleven minutes later and nobody was hurt. **No box on that page because there was no box to rule, and no person anywhere on it, and there is not going to be.** The person who took that share off was the person holding it, was thirty-eight, and nobody has ever asked her whether she was willing to and nobody is going to. A nurse of thirty-four has said in a room that the difference between that woman and a front is that a front cannot say no in about nine words about a share, **and she is not on the page and her name is not going to be on it.**
5. **Ch 150, the week of week 46 — the close, and the final image reached item by item.** The grey transit sequence card on a shelf above a bench with a boy's handwriting in the fifth line, two names against a review date in the spring, one taken off the whole row with a knife in a fish market, and the taker's name in the fourth column; the original in the fourth pocket of the tool roll with the fifth line still blank and nobody having ever asked him to fill it in; the list in a plastic sleeve on the wall with a fifth line and his initials meaning *not done*, **and no document in this city requires it to be there**; four miles away the form with the pencil box, no reference number, a date, and a woman of thirty-one with a diary told twice this term that she has no standing; and a leaflet, about four thousand of them, naming no technique and no person, saying a thing that about eleven thousand people paid for, **and somebody has to be able to say the sentence and the sentence has no name on it and a man of fifty-one who said it in a market is the only person in the city who has ever been paid for it.** **The master is in an inside pocket on a chair, four feet from the original in the tool roll, one fifth line written and one blank, and the decision has not been made in a term and a half.**
6. **Ch 150 — Roy Amiss, fifty-one, is on the page and is not paid.** A frame and a stick and the leaflet on his own table at home; he asks whether the thing on the table is about him and is given nine words that are true and do not answer the question he asked, and both of them know it inside four seconds. **He says nobody rang him before or after and that he is not saying it to anybody.** Nobody rang him before or after that either, and **he is owed a thing that is not a leaflet and there is no form for it, and it carries into Volume 04.**
7. **Ch 150 — Rafi Pell's worse answer, and the canon on his card.** **Not being the reason it got a name on it is not a thing you can hand to somebody else at a door, and nine people in this city have said that and it did not do anything.** The third practice is still his, he has still not signed it, and either of the other two can refuse him in about nine words. **The grey card has two names on it and neither of them is Marek Senn; the word *variant* is not forgiven and nobody has asked about it; nobody took a vote about him and nobody asked him to stop.**
8. **Ch 150 — the price's nine words, once, and the last sentence of the volume.** **Nobody is going to say whose practice this is** is said out loud once in Chapter 150, in a car park, to nobody, and not said again; **its load book deliberately does not print the sentence a second time**, because a thing that gets written twice in a book gets said twice in a room. **The last line of the volume is that about eleven people a week are still going to be taught that a release waits for a man in a lanyard that this case has just made unnecessary, and the line after it is that nobody says so.**
9. **Ch 150 — the seventh session and the microphone.** It is in Crown Terrace in two weeks, which keeps the four-week spacing from Chapter 144, and **both of the two people printed on it as a job are him, and the standing problem is not resolved and no rule about who may be asked is invented.** A man of about fifty-one **has asked in writing, in a room, in advance, for the microphone to be switched off for the one item that is his, and nobody said no, and nobody has told him that asking is what makes it permanent.**

## The four things in this volume that are load-bearing and must not be undone

1. **The specification runs to FOUR SECTIONS and had TWO LINES, and a funded THIRD LINE of nine words was minuted on the Wednesday of week thirty-seven: *the coverage of a supervised confirmation where the holder is not a named party to the share*.** Quote it as a third line and never as a ninth line. A fourth number, four hundred and one, is in the same minute and belongs to a company's postbox. **The fund's committee does not meet again in this volume and nothing in it is settled by a committee, a minute, a notice, a report or a leaflet.**
2. **There is exactly one box in this city for the person who removed a share, and it is in pencil, on one page, on a counter, with three words over it in a clerk's hand.** **There is no reference number on it, it is not on a system, it has no post attached to it, and it was empty on the day it was made.** A Volume 04 writer may put a name in it and may not give it a reference number, move it onto a system, attach a post to it, or have a district adopt it.
3. **The relay was staged once, in Chapter 136, and it cost a person who was not asked first and who stopped it.** It may not be staged again in any form, the carrier may not be asked again, and **no later page may imply that it got easier.** The observation arrived with a kerb on the outside of a corner and about four stops and a Thursday on it, and somebody downstream could tell and was right. **This contradicts `outline/series.md`'s Volume 04 power state, which says *Stage 2, with the first sustained relay*, and that line has to be re-based before Volume 04 is drafted.**
4. **The two fifth lines are the same five words and not the same sentence**, and the only question that separates a counterfeit from a company card is **who the technique is waiting for.** A courier's third line on the back of an invoice is not a fifth line.

## What Volume 04 inherits, dated

- **Week 46 = Mon 208 – Sun 214. The last dated scene in Volume 03 is the Monday night of week forty-six in a repair shop in Lattice Ward. Week 47 begins the next volume.**
- **The next Exchange is the Wednesday of week 48** on the four-week spacing 16, 20, 24, 28, 32, 36, 40, 44, 48. **Bea Nunn will not open the book on a Friday. The book is at forty-four lines, the fortieth is three years old, line forty-four has a hole in it, and it is not a register.** *Correction made in this pass: `state/current.md` carried a line saying the next Exchange was the Wednesday of week forty-five. That was wrong, it contradicted the authoritative batch brief and the four-week spacing, and no Exchange is staged in Chapter 149 or Chapter 150.*
- **The Friday rota: four days a week at grade two, two Fridays at grade three, on a card in date or an exception recorded and renewed in a box that says *No reason given by holder*.** About eleven people a week, seven on the rota and about four not on it. **The number is not a rate, is on the back of a woman's own card, and is not on a form anywhere.**
- **The plate says 2-14 over a room that is 2-07, about four hundred consent leaflets are printed 2-07, the fault ticket is closed, and the room is on a shelf.** The only route out of that corridor is a counter and a diary.
- **The counterfeit: four hundred and thirty-two cards in about nine places in one district, a hundred and forty-one pounds in a bag under a till with no name and no address, no mechanism in this city that can take a card back off anybody for about seven weeks, and a boy of nineteen who has now run the machine once.**
- **The master: an inside pocket on a chair in Lattice Ward, a photocopy of a photocopy, a fifth line in a hand that got smaller towards the end, and no decision.** The original is in the fourth pocket of the tool roll with the fifth line still blank. **The drawer under the bench holds five things, four of them other people's sentences, one of them a letter from the Continuity Office that declines in writing with a count of 4,106 / 940 / 211 and four assessors and whose own last line says it should not be produced without that office being asked first. There is no proceeding.**
- **The box number and the four-letter post town are in two hands, one of them a foreman of fifty-eight with a telephone on a wall, and nobody has rung it.**
- **The word is uncorrected, is told to nobody, and is now a condition of paid work.** The consequence has changed form rather than being fixed: he has talked over three people in a fortnight and has failed to hear one.
- **The panel problem: two of the five people on a panel are the same man, chosen by a system out of two job sheets, and there is no mechanism in this city that can tell a man doing a thing from a man being asked about one.** Unresolved, said publicly three times.
- **The seven is still seven, the four things that are not the seven are still four, and the word *pattern* does not appear in Volume 03 and may not appear in Volume 04.**
- **Open, unpaid, and not to be closed by a document:** the licensor, still blank; the second visitor column's five other words; the bit of pale card in a gate hut, and the fact that a room in Crown Terrace knows a man of sixty-one has it; the unsigned sheet and its four correct steps; the printer's customer; the woman of thirty-nine and her page of her own; the four hundred and one replies; the nine words wrong in a minute; the fifth room and who takes the name out; the unlabelled bay, the list of named holders, the boy at the control post, Delia Frost's landing, Nkechi Osei's Mondays; **Roy Amiss's unringed telephone**; and the hundred and forty-one pounds in a bag.
- **Relationship: Series milestone 3 is spent and may not be spent again. Milestone 4, *public and private friction*, belongs to Volumes 05 and 06 and nothing in Volume 04 should pre-empt it.** The bench is not reopened, Chapter 102's Tuesday is not revisited, the Chapter 7 page stays closed, and Volume 03's resolution was done by a woman of thirty-one who is not her.

## The canon decisions of the run that wrote Chapters 149 and 150

1. **A NEW PERSON ON THE RECORD AND NOT TO BE RE-CAST: Petra Lindqvist, fifty-three, a clerk at a counter in Bower Street with nineteen years behind her, who ruled a box in pencil because she was asked to and not because she was persuaded, and who has not mentioned the woman of thirty-one since.** **Ianthe Roke, thirty-one**, was already named in `outline/volume-03.md` and is now the woman the volume's resolution belongs to.
2. **ROY AMISS IS FIFTY-ONE AND PETER AMADO IS ABOUT FIFTY-ONE AND THEY ARE TWO DIFFERENT MEN**, and a writer must keep them apart: one is owed a telephone call that nobody makes, the other is paid a fee to say a sentence about who owns a practice.
3. **THE RESOLUTION'S WORD COUNT IS RECORDED RATHER THAN ASSERTED, and the brief's four-hundred-word figure is not met and the chapter is not cut to reach it.** The brief asked for the resolution in about four hundred words and the chapter is **2,606**. In Chapter 149 **the procedure itself is 186 words** — the refusal, the ask, the pencil, three words over a box, and the paragraph saying there is no reference number and it is not a system — **the two cost statements the brief also requires are 194**, and the chapter's prose is **1,563 words**, the shortest in the batch, with a **1,043-word** load-book entry on top of it. **The chapter also has to carry a counter with about two hundred forms on it, four stacks, the plate, the district nurse's nine seconds, a clerk who has been told twice, the box in pencil and a woman who is not thanked, and those do not fit in four hundred words; a repair pass that shortened the prose to satisfy a heading would have destroyed the volume's whole resolution to satisfy its own margin.** The first draft of the load-book block was headed *the resolution, and the whole of this volume, in about four hundred words* — the brief's instruction to the writer sitting inside a first-person entry, and false on its own terms — **and the heading is now *the resolution, and the whole of this volume*, which is the house idiom of batches 0001, 0002 and 0004.**
4. **THE BRIEF'S RULE ABOUT NINE WORDS IS HONOURED EXACTLY ONCE MORE.** The price's nine words appear in Chapter 148 twice, in the prose and the load book, as the review repair pass left them, and **in Chapter 150 once, spoken in a car park, and its load book does not print them.**
5. **THE NARRATOR'S OWN CAR-PARK AND SHOP DURATIONS ARE STATED EXACT, matching Chapter 142, and the volume's calendar nouns are unchanged: no day number, no month-name, no month-date, no day-date and no year in either chapter.** One month-name was in the first draft of Chapter 150 and was cut, and the sentence it was in was also wrong: it dated Rafi Pell's yard conversation to a month, and Chapter 147's load book puts it **in the week of the forty-second**, which is what it now says.
6. **NO FOURTH SHINGLE, NO REPEATED PARAGRAPH, NO `* * *` MARKER, NO UNCLOSED MARKER.** The docket refrain, the *best document anybody in this case has written* line, the paid sentence and Rafi's remembered question were all reworded in Chapters 149 and 150 where they had reproduced an earlier chapter at the eighty-character threshold, and **the batch now has no cross-chapter duplication involving either new chapter**, and no duplicated paragraph anywhere.

## The canon decisions of the second review repair pass on batch 0005

**A second review was run on Chapters 149 and 150 and on the hand-off documents after the volume was closed. It returned seven findings and all seven are applied. No scene was cut, no plot was moved, no character was changed, the day map, the load-book run, the three doors, the relay, the carrier, the fourth condition and the master are untouched, and Chapters 141 to 148 were not opened at all.** The two blocking ones were a continuity break and a leaked instruction.

1. **ROY AMISS'S ADMISSION WEEK IS CORRECTED AND IT IS LOAD-BEARING, because the wrong week had reached the character file as well as the chapter.** Canon, from Chapter 107 in his own words — *they brought me in on the Thursday* — and from the discharge chapter: **he was brought in on a Thursday, and he went down to a car at eleven o'clock on the Thursday of week thirty-four.** Chapter 150 had said the Thursday of week thirty-eight, which is Talia Venn's clinic day and the day he happens to be in the room in Chapter 135, and had put his admission and his discharge on the same week, which is not possible against an eleven-day stay. **He is now brought in on a Thursday in week thirty and let out on the Thursday afternoon of week thirty-four, in the chapter and in `state/character-state.md`.** The arithmetic that would make an eleven-day stay and a Thursday-to-Thursday discharge agree is **not settled by this pass and is not printed anywhere**; the two firm facts are printed and the soft one is not.
2. **A DRAFTING INSTRUCTION IS OUT OF THE MANUSCRIPT.** The Chapter 149 load-book block was headed *the resolution, and the whole of this volume, in about four hundred words*, which is the brief's instruction to the writer sitting inside a first-person entry, and which was false because the block it heads is 186 words. **The heading is now *the resolution, and the whole of this volume***, the house idiom of batches 0001, 0002 and 0004. **No drafting instruction, word target, chapter count or rule quotation is to appear in a load book again**: a load book is a document inside the story and it is read by one man who is keeping it against himself.
3. **THE MEASURED FIGURES ARE CORRECTED, AND ONE OF THE THREE NO LONGER BEATS THE FIGURE IT INHERITED.** The batch is **29,868 words, 306 raw instances of *about* at 10.25 per thousand, 203 true hedges at 6.80, and 287 bold spans at 9.61 in a range of 7.54 to 10.73.** The figures previously printed in the batch summary, in `state/current.md` and in the Volume 04 prompt — 29,845 words, 296 raw at 9.92, 168 true at 5.63, 287 spans at 9.62, a worst chapter of 10.91 and a lowest of 7.96 — **are withdrawn.** Two separate causes: **the raw count came from a lowercase-literal matcher that could not see the eleven capitalised *About* instances opening load-book labels**, and **the true-hedge count came from an implementation that required a number word to be followed by a counted noun, so *about nine* and *about four of the eleven* fell out.** The rule is now printed with its word lists, because **a word list described in prose is not a rule a later writer can re-derive, and that has now cost Volume 03 figures twice.** **The honest consequence is recorded rather than tidied: the raw and the bold figures beat batch 0004's 10.77 and 10.72, and the true-hedge figure does not, at 6.80 against 5.65.**
4. **THE WORKING PATTERN IS FOUR DAYS ON GRADE TWO AND TWO FRIDAYS, and the correction this batch claimed to have made had not reached the hand-off.** The batch summary listed it among four errors "all real and all fixed" while `state/chapter-summaries.md` still carried *five days on grade two and a Friday* in the Chapter 150 block. **A hand-off document is where the next writer reads, and a correction that is not propagated is not a correction.**
5. **THE COUNTERFEIT IS FOUR HUNDRED AND THIRTY-TWO CARDS AND NOT ONE.** Chapter 150 said *about nine places in one district still have a card out of that counter*, which is two orders of magnitude out and contradicts both the canon entry and load-book entry 153 in the same file. **It now reads *four hundred and thirty-two cards are in about nine places in one district*, with both canonical hedges kept.**
6. **A GRAMMAR SLIP IS FIXED:** *the Thursday of week forty-fourth* in Chapter 149 is *of week forty-four*.
7. **THE MASTER IS IN THE CLOSE'S PROSE AND NOT ONLY IN ITS LOAD BOOK.** The hard rule is that the master does not leave the pocket, and Chapter 150 had a jacket over the back of a chair and never said what was in it. **It is now one clause in the manuscript's own idiom — the master was in the inside pocket of it, and nobody has asked him for it in a term and a half.** The outline's final image does not include the master, so this confirms a hard rule and does not change the image. **The chapter still does not decide anything about it, no document chooses, and nobody decides it for him.**

**And the volume's word total moves with the batch: 182,422 words in fifty chapters, batch 0005 being 29,868.** Every figure in `workspace/volume-03/batch-0005/SUMMARY.md`, `state/current.md`, this file and `workspace/continuation/next-0006/PROMPT.md` is now the one the ten files produce.

**And one correction reached back into a closed batch, which is recorded here because a closed summary is still read.** The wrong week in Chapter 150 came from `workspace/volume-03/batch-0004/SUMMARY.md`, which had already said that the man of fifty-one went in on the Thursday of week thirty-eight and came out on the Thursday afternoon. **That line is corrected in place, in the same pass, with the correction printed beside it rather than made silently**, and no prose in batch 0004 was opened. **The general lesson, and it is the reason this is written down: a summary is a hand-off document, so an error in one is not a closed error — it is a loaded one, waiting for the next writer who reads it before writing.**

## The canon decisions of the review repair pass on batch 0005, in seven lines, and a later writer quotes these rather than re-deriving them

1. **THE BATCH'S FIGURES ARE 26,053 WORDS, 310 RAW *ABOUT* AT 11.90 PER THOUSAND, 211 TRUE HEDGES AT 8.10, AND 157 BOLD SPANS AT 6.03 WITH A WORST CHAPTER OF 8.33,** and the per-chapter load-book overlap series is 23, 29, 21, 2, 15, 38, 29, 52, 41, 49. **Every one of these was re-run on the ten files after the last edit. The first version of the batch summary carried 25,895 and 30.2 and both were stale, and this is the sixth time this volume has published a figure that had not been re-measured on its own files.**
2. **A WITHIN-BATCH DUPLICATE PASS IS PART OF THE METHOD FROM HERE ON.** The eighty-character sweep looks backwards at earlier batches and cannot compare the ten new files with each other. It missed a 319-character paragraph Chapter 200 had copied from Chapter 196 and three more pairs at 66 to 84 characters. All four are reworded with the same facts and the batch is clean at eighty and at full.
3. **IN CHAPTER 197 THE MAN WHO AUTHORISED THE PURGE IS IVEN SORE, NAMED, AND THE MAN HE SPEAKS TO IS THE FIELD SERVICE ENGINEER OF FIFTY-ONE WITH A TOOL BAG.** One corridor, two men, fifty-eight and fifty-one, the coat on one and the tool bag on the other. **Iven Sore is named in Chapters 155, 194 and 197 and what is new in Chapter 197 is the first person and the admission, not the name.**
4. **THE EXCHANGE'S RUN IS FIVE SITTINGS — THE WEDNESDAYS OF WEEKS FORTY-EIGHT, FIFTY-TWO, FIFTY-SIX, SIXTY AND SIXTY-FOUR — WITH THREE WEDNESDAYS IN BETWEEN THEM AND FOUR GAPS, FOUR WEEKS APART.** The book is at forty-four lines, no caller has signed, and the next sitting is the Wednesday of week sixty-eight, which is after Chapter 200. **Chapter 192's prose now states the five, the three and the four; the figure was never in doubt and the sentence around it was.**
5. **IN CHAPTER 195 THE NAME THAT IS NOT SAID IS THE NAME ON THE PAGE AND NOT THE NAME OF THE WOMAN WHO RECOGNISED THE HAND.** Sera Quill is named in the chapter and has always been named; the authorship is not given and does not hint at itself.
6. **NOTHING ELSE MOVED.** No chapter was restarted, no scene was rewritten from the top, no day in the ten-day map changed, no load-book entry changed number, the run is still 194 to 203, and the prose that was good is where it was.
7. **`workspace/volume-04/batch-0005/VOLUME-CLOSE.md` IS AN ACCOUNT AND NOT A DISPATCHABLE PROMPT,** because the runner selects a phase by a `PROMPT.md` and a marker and cannot see it. **This block is the live state. It sits above the `VOLUME 06 — MOVEMENT I` block, which is correct in substance and is superseded on the three points named in it. **1. THE NAME. Talia Venn is now named in the prose of Chapters 252, 253, 255, 258, 259 and 260, and in the load books of FIVE OF THOSE SIX — 253, 255, 258, 259 and 260 — and Chapter 252's entry does not name her, because her only appearance in that chapter is in the body at line 21 and the entry records a day that did not turn on her. ## What is fixed by these ten chapters and a later writer quotes rather than re-derives

1. **THE CLAUSE IS PRINTED AND IT IS NOT HIDDEN, AND THE NAME IS NOT SPOKEN.** An emergency procedure with a number and a date, on a wall, in a binder in a district practice standards office in District 3, requiring a practice card to be issued only if it carries a beat that performs the boundary. **The reference is PS-3-11(c) and it is dated eleven years and it has never been withdrawn and nothing in that binder has been withdrawn in nineteen years, because withdrawing one is a thing a person writes on it. The reference is in the load books of Chapters 252, 255 and 256 and is NOT in the prose of any of the ten, and a later writer must not put it in prose.** It is referred to as *the printed rule*, *the standard*, or *the last approved civic sequence*, and by nobody as a name. **There is no conspiracy, no villain holding a secret and nobody to catch. About four hundred competent people in this city can invoke it and all of them are doing their jobs, and the horror of the volume is that a competent person invoking a printed rule is correct.**
2. **THE PUBLIC BODY IS A ROOM.** A hearing set for the Monday of week ninety-two, one morning, in a room in Crown Terrace, about nine chairs, and the person who will chair it has an office that signs about eleven of a kind a year. **A chair of forty-nine is a person with an ordinary job and is not a post, and she is NOT NAMED in these ten chapters and a later writer may name her. A person whose job it is to ask questions is not a mechanism. The word *Assembly* does not appear in the prose of this volume.**
3. **THE OBJECT OF THE VOLUME.** A page of about forty-one people in one district that cannot be closed from the outside, about nine of whom were telephoned before anything happened and one of whom was not and was safe; a box ruled in pencil on a counter in Bower Street by a clerk of fifty-three with nineteen years behind her, holding one name, with no reference number, not on a system, not a form, may not take a second name, and may not be emptied; about four hundred and one replies in a company's general enquiries box, of which about nine of the first forty-one got the company answer, leaving about three hundred and sixty unread in that building and read by a clerk of forty-four; about nine hundred doors in four buildings carrying a number off a different line of the same company's list, put on by a field service engineer of fifty-one who cannot change one of them; a wall plate in one corridor that says two-fourteen over a room that is two-oh-seven, with about four hundred leaflets printed two-oh-seven in the rack and a fault ticket closed with the correct number in the building's own typeface by a man doing the job properly, which changed nothing; and the phrase *patient identifiers, if available*.
4. **A COMPANY CARD WITH FIVE PARTS NAMED AND THE BEAT THAT PERFORMS THE BOUNDARY MISSING.** A starting hold, ordered steps, a point of release, a printed box saying what the practice must not touch, and a statement that an aftertaste is normal. **Five printings exist: the hold, the steps and the release are one typeface in all five, and the boundary box is a different typeface at half a point larger in the fifth, in circulation about eleven years, with a revision column carrying no note of withdrawal, amendment, reason or reviewer anywhere on it. The card is not hidden and the company is not the author of anything.** The ending beat of a practice is on no card, has never been on a card, and is not in the box on the post by the door in nine company training rooms; it is a spoken word said by a person whose job it is to say it.
5. **THE INVOCATION AND WHAT IT COST.** Read off a wall binder in about nine seconds on the Wednesday morning of week ninety by a clerk of forty-four, who said before doing it that she did not want to be the one who said it out loud, that this was the reason she had to, and that in about four years it would appear in a newspaper with a photograph of the person who did it on it. **Anybody in that office may invoke it on a Monday without asking anybody. About nine hundred cards across about eleven sites came out of use in about four days, and the number is a number of objects and not of people. The checking was done by a man of thirty-four who buys training, on his own time, on four days his employer does not know he took, thanked at nine sites and asked at two whether he was from a company. Nobody thanked her. She is owed nothing by anybody in this case. It changed nothing.**
6. **WHAT THE FOUR DAYS FOUND AND WHAT THEY DID NOT.** Found: the two-typeface card in nine of about eleven sites; four of the nine locally reprinted by a printer told he was correcting a misprint, in one of which a man of fifty-eight with a bad hip printed two hundred himself above a fish market, for nothing, out of a habit he cannot stop; about nine people on no rota across four sites, two of whom have never been asked whether they want to be there, one being a man of fifty-one with a bad wrist who attends on a Friday and is not on the list. Not found: anything about the eleven a week, which is the finding, stated first by a man of twenty-nine who works on lifts in about nine words and not improved on.
7. **A HEARING WITH TWO FAULTS IN ITS ITEM, ONE OF WHICH CANNOT BE UNDONE.** The item asks whether a practice can be required to show that any person in it may stop at any time. Fault one: the clerk of forty-four who invoked the standard is **now seconded to the secretariat of the standing committee that is to hear the case**, because a committee with an item needs a person to prepare it, and the chair has not been told, and she is the only person in either building who knows what it cost to say the number out loud in a room. Fault two: a practice cannot show that a person may stop, because the only means of showing it is a record of who stopped, and a record of who stopped is a column, and a column is the one thing that kind of room does not have, **and a hearing where the only available answer is no is a way of producing a finding and is not a hearing.** Found and said once, standing up, in a room and not in a corridor, by a practice standards officer of sixty with nineteen years in the office, who is right, who is thanked by nobody, and who has said once in nineteen years that she is right every time she opens her mouth and that this is the difficulty and not the qualification.
8. **THE REFUSAL, AND THE REASON UNDER THE REASON.** A woman of twenty-four refused, before any question was put to her, to be the person who reads the standard aloud in a room where it decides something. Reason given the room: if she says it, the only thing anybody can argue with becomes her; the rule is printed, numbered, dated and invokable by any competent person on a Monday, and once said by her it acquires a face, and a face can be cross-examined and a number on a wall cannot. Reason given herself, given unprompted and labelled as the real one: she is tired; she has been asked to be findable three times in a term and a half and has said no three times and this is the fourth; tiredness is not a reason and she intends to use it; she would like to be somewhere that is not the answer. **A man of twenty-two argued for about four minutes and was wrong, and a first-year of nineteen said so in nine words from the end chair with her boots off, and he accepted it in about four seconds, did not say he would not improve on it, did not repeat it and did not argue. Nobody thanked him for not arguing. This is series milestone 4 paid in its series form, in public, in a room, and it is paid and it is not resolved.**
9. **A COMPANY ASKED TO COME AND SEE THE ROOM AND WAS REFUSED IN WRITING.** A woman of about forty-three whose company supplies about eleven of the sites asked for about forty minutes and came with one other person and said, unprompted, that she has a photograph of that counter in a bag in a car and has looked at it more than her own. A first-year of nineteen refused in about nine seconds: a room which admits a company becomes a room that can be quoted, and there is no way in this city to make a person take it back out of a sentence. **The cost of the no: there is no name over the door, no committee, no minute, no officer and no post, and a written refusal requires a person at the bottom of it, and the only person anybody in that building could name to a stranger is him. He wrote about four sentences on her company's own notepaper at the counter in about four minutes, signed it, and read it back to the room before it was sealed, which nobody asked him to do. It is now in a company in another district and cannot be taken back because he wrote it, and the first line on that hardboard, that the room will not keep anything it cannot take back, does not apply to it and he did not claim that it does. The ring binder did not take it.** She was given four figures out loud once and wrote them on the back of her own company's card and left no copy, and named her own cost: a file note recording a declined approach, which inside her own employer is a thing she can be found about, and a preference for writing it herself rather than have it written about her by the person above her. She is owed nothing, is not a villain, and her company sends nothing further this term.
10. **THE FOURTEENTH LINE ON THE HARDBOARD, AND THE DIFFERENCE BETWEEN A LINE AND A RULE.** Fourteen words, in the hand of a woman of twenty-four, unsigned, with a space left where a name would go: **that she is not going to be the person who is asked about this room.** She named the cost out loud before the mark — that her name is then on a surface in a building with a plate on the outside of it carrying a telephone number and no name, that such a plate is how about eleven things reached that door in about a term, that in about four years somebody may photograph that hardboard and her handwriting will be on it and she cannot take it off, and that she has said no to being findable three times in a term and a half and that this was not one of the three. Nobody improved on the cost, nobody said it was worth it, nobody said it was not, and nobody was thanked. **The wall is fourteen lines in eight hands and nine names. The first line is a cook's and is still the first line. It was the first line in this case that was not read out before it was written, and the reason is on the page.** A first-year of nineteen then put the two objects on the page in one minute without the narrator explaining either: **a rule has a number and a date and hangs on a wall in a building with a great deal of paper in it and any competent person may pick it up on a Monday; a line costs its writer something said out loud, binds nobody including its writer, can be lifted with a thumb in front of nine people, and is evidence of nothing in any proceeding, there being none.** The woman of twenty-four said she did not know the difference on Wednesday and had believed for a term and a half that the two were the same thing. **The hardboard is on two nails, carries no date, no number, no reference and nothing at the top, and is not the board over the door, which carries four words in chalk in the hand of a woman of about sixty-eight and one unremovable grey ghost of four in a sign-writer's hand, and is not the wall list in its plastic sleeve in the repair shop in Lattice Ward. Three objects and none merged.**
11. **THE RATE, THE SECOND TIME, AND THE TWO FIGURES OF NINE HUNDRED.** Nine, ten and eleven a minute, uncorrected, and **eleven for about four minutes at about ten past four on the Friday of week ninety, the second time in about a term and a half, the first having been while about eleven buildings were being cleared in standing water. He told nobody, then or now, and at about ten on a service road he said the number out loud once to an empty road, which is the only time in six volumes he has said it to anything that cannot answer, and he has now done it twice.** Two figures of about nine hundred exist in this case and are not the same nine hundred: cards withdrawn from about eleven sites, and doors in four buildings carrying a number off a different line of the same list. **A man of fifty-one with a tool bag established that four of the eleven sites are buildings he has worked in, that no person in this city can tell the two sets apart in under about four minutes, and that this is the second time he has asked for the fact to be written down. Nobody wrote it down.**
12. **THE RING BINDER HOLDS EIGHT ITEMS AND HAS NOT BEEN OPENED.** The eighth went in on the Monday of week eighty-nine, put there by a woman of about thirty who was not asked, who is not thanked, and whose page nobody in that room has read and **nobody in this volume is going to read.** It is folded once and it is the size of half a sheet of typing paper and the corners have gone soft. Any person may take the binder off the shelf and read it standing up; any person who wrote one of the eight may take that page out and nobody will stop them and nobody will be told. **No page has been taken out.**
13. **WHAT THE WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY DID, WHICH IS ALL SHE DID.** She has been in that room on every working day since the Friday of week sixty-nine because it is warm. She folded a page, carried it to a shelf, put it in a ring binder, shut it and sat down, and said nine words addressed to the room. **She has now done three things in that room in nineteen weeks, two of them sentences said to people and this one a sentence said to a shelf. She is not named, is not a category, is not counted by anything but a count, is not described by age or district or occupation, has not been asked a question by anybody, has not been thanked, has not been apologised to, and an apology would have been a question and would still be one. She is the person a fortnight of unpaid heating was paid for and she is still in the chair nearest the door.**
14. **THE HEATING.** Off at seven in the morning on the Monday of week eighty-three, unpaid, and **on its fifty-ninth day at Chapter 260.** No fund, collection, subscription, grant, list, minute, notice or appeal exists and none was proposed by anybody in these ten chapters. **A cook of twenty-seven put the gas ring on at about half past eight, as she has done for about fourteen weeks before that at the same time on the same mornings, and on the Monday of week eighty-nine said out loud that she has asked three times, will not ask a fourth, that this is not a refusal but a Tuesday she is not going to spend again, and that this was the fourth time she had said a thing of that shape against a promise not to say a fifth.** It comes back in Chapter 285, the Wednesday of week ninety-nine, by a landlord, and the notice arrives in Chapter 290.
15. **THE BOUNDARIES, ALL OF THEM, UNCHANGED.** No teaching, demonstration, assessment, assistance or relay by him in any of the ten, and the fourth condition is neither quoted nor paraphrased in any of them, and **a room off a service road is not a practice in that department and the reason is not that he is the exception.** The word *carrier* appears nowhere and the relay is not staged, rehearsed or previewed. No message arrives from anybody. The licensor is unnamed and the field is blank and no authorship of a technique bundle is identified. **Nine keys are needed and two exist and the count was not said aloud in any of the ten, because it was said aloud for the first time in front of the two people who own the other two and saying it again in a different room is a refrain and not a discovery.** The master is in the fourth pocket of a tool roll in another building with its fifth line blank and nothing was decided about it. The card in a rail is a third object and is never confused with the other two in one sentence. The box in pencil in Bower Street is untouched and holds one name. The Exchange does not fall in this batch; the book stands at forty-six lines and the telephone count at forty-four and the book is two lines ahead of the telephone throughout, and Bea Nunn does not open it on a Friday and no Friday in this batch is an Exchange day. **Asha Reed is the only nineteen-year-old in the case. The two men of fifty-eight remain two men and the printer of fifty-eight is a woman and is a third person. The young woman of twenty-one in the Exchange back room is not in this batch. The standing question is not stated in any of the ten chapters, is referred to in eight of the ten load books, and no rule about who may be asked was invented and no number is attached to one.**
