## What Chapters 231–240 added to the record, in the order it is established

1. **Ch 231, the Friday of week 79 — a man of thirty-four who works nights, two true sentences, and a company telephoning a plate.** Eleven jobs, ten in date, and two of them signed off by a district man of thirty-eight who said out loud that he had not called it the fault and that this is the third caller that month to be right about a door closer, the other two being also right. **A man of thirty-four who has been in that room four times in about seven weeks, has never brought anything, and once turned the kettle plug off after it had been on six hours, said two sentences he had been holding since the spring: that at about four in the morning he is alone with a pump in a building feeding about nine hundred doors, that at some point in every shift he takes his hand off it and says a thing out loud, that in about nine years nothing has ever happened, and that he has told nobody and came hoping somebody would say something; and that he would like somebody to watch him do it, has wanted that for about two terms and about four hundred times in a corridor, there is nobody to ask, and he is not asking for it that night. He distinguished the two himself, unprompted: his is a wish to be watched, the sentence going round this city is a need for a person to be present, one of the two is about him and the other about a stranger, and he has never been in a position to tell the difference and neither has anybody in a lanyard.** Nobody improved on him and a cook of twenty-seven said one word that was not agreement. **At about half past eight a company telephoned the number on the plate, and a letting clerk of thirty-nine with a ring file gave the sentence about a vacant frontage that she has now given to five sets of people, was told it was not about the unit, and said hold on — the fourth time, the first having been the Tuesday in the week of seventy-two, the second a sister of sixty-four in the week of seventy-six, the third a person of thirty-four in the week of seventy-eight. She said the word *vacant* about a unit open every working day for twelve weeks, asked for no name, has all four calls on a sheet of her own that she has said three times she will not bring to that room, does not know what she has done, has not been told, and is not thanked.** A woman of about thirty was in that room when a company was told the unit was vacant.
2. **Ch 232, the Monday of week 80 — a man of thirty-seven with a sheet of a company's paper, and the sentence about money nobody could answer.** He administers property for a company that appears in a schedule of title on that row, has held the job six years, signs the line that moves the money, and has asked twice why the first year is free and been refused twice with the same true sentence: a lease matter is a ring file in another building. **He said the true thing first, unasked, and it was that there is no money in this room — he went round a shutter, a counter, nine chairs, a window, a kettle, a bell-push, a piece of hardboard and the floor and found none in any of them — and that he has signed the paper saying the first year is free every year for six years and has never been told why. He then said the part nobody asked for: in about four weeks somebody in a building he has never been inside is going to find a unit open twelve weeks and cost nothing, he will not be the person asked, and he does not know what he would say.** Two people in the room tried to answer and neither produced an answer, because the only person who knows has said the same true thing about a frontage five times, is not thanked, has not been asked and was not asked that morning. **His reason for coming in person is his own and is that he signs the money and wanted to have stood in the room before he moved any, and that in about nine years he has signed about nine arrangements on that row and been inside one.** He left the sheet face down on the counter, the first object anybody has put there which cannot be picked up and put down again in about nine seconds.
3. **Ch 233, the Wednesday of week 80 — the ninth sitting, the book shut, and the first thing Bea Nunn said about money.** Nine sittings, eight gaps of four weeks, the tally of sittings at which nothing has been added is eight and a half, and the rule was given whole and unimproved: a book of what a caller said cannot be written by the people who did not call. **The rule has been given at all nine sittings and asked for eight times in nineteen years, and the ninth was given to a room that had not asked for it; neither figure is to be converted into the other. Nine chairs, and the ninth turned to face the wall since the previous sitting, put there by a young woman of twenty-one who began coming to that room at fifteen, never asked to move it, never named in it and given no reason; she is not the first-year of nineteen, she is not Asha Reed, and there is one nineteen-year-old in this case and it is the other one.** **She said, before she sat down and before anybody in that room had referred to a company or to money, that she has been asked the same question before, by a supplier, eleven years ago and about four years ago, and that she has told nobody in that room what is in that room since she came into it and the single person who knew is dead; that he brought a figure to the door, asked if the book could be bought, was given the standing refusal, asked again on the way out, was given it again, and did not return; and that her reason is not rudeness, not cleverness and not being right, but that there is nothing in that room to buy and she would not let him find that out from her.** The telephone count moved from thirty-five to thirty-seven, announced unasked at about twenty past ten, and she has announced it fourteen times of which nine correspond to the nine Wednesdays this book holds and the first five predate it, and she performed the subtraction aloud, refused to repeat it, has done it three times, and stated that the figure is about her and has never been about the room and that the room predates the book. One of the two who rang did not come and she has not telephoned her and declined to give her spring reason a fourth time. **The ninth reason that room has given for not opening a book, and the first that is about one person: a woman telephoned in the spring about something she had said, telephoned again to ask whether it had been written down, and cannot be answered without opening the book in front of nine people, which would publish the one thing she asked for. Bea Nunn has not looked, and said that in nineteen years she has answered every question put to her in that room and has one she cannot, and that the question is whether a person she spoke to on a telephone is owed an answer.** A woman of thirty-four who runs a kitchen with about eleven people in it came, was refused with the standing answer, thanked the woman and came, was told she could have signed and that the line would be the forty-sixth, and said no — because nobody asked her; was told she would have said yes; and agreed, and said that is precisely why nobody asked. **The book is at forty-five lines, nobody signed anything in this chapter, and the volume's figure of forty-six is a later problem and is not this chapter's.** Two items at four and nine minutes were taken and neither was written down. A man of twenty-two sat in a corner for about two hours, said nothing, and was not thanked.
4. **Ch 234, the Thursday of week 80 — the offer, in about nine minutes, and the terms are good.** A man of forty-one gave his name, which was Vey, and nobody wrote it down, and he kept his coat on. **He said the true thing about money first and unasked: there is no money in this room; he has been looking for four months for a thing that does not exist; and that is the only reason a man of his age is on a service road on a Thursday.** He then read one sheet in about nine minutes: one figure and five numbered clauses and no name on it. **Heat, light, water and the two working sockets, quarterly from the day the room opened, payable whether or not anybody comes through the door in a quarter; nine chairs and nine more when there are nine who want them, a floor that does not slope, a back door with nothing off its top edge, and a sounder audible from the service road; nothing said in that room to be printed, photographed, described, put on a course or used in any material, in writing and not as a conversation; no person in that room gives a name to anybody, and so no account and no cheque name, the money going into a pool already standing behind about nine hundred doors; nobody from that company in the room more than once a month, on invitation, leaving immediately on request, and never again after a second.** And a sixth condition given aloud and not on the page: refusal inside a fortnight, with nothing owed, nothing asked, nothing printed and no return, supported by one line in his own file which he declined to describe and which is the only line in it about that room. **A cook of twenty-seven answered the sounder clause first, having put two screws into that sounder herself on a Thursday night with nine people watching and been thanked for it never. A first-year of nineteen then found what is actually wrong with the sheet in about four minutes: money is a name, it is the only name on the sheet and the only one that cannot be refused, a number will be needed to move the money and the number will be that room's because the other one exists already, and a number is findable where a leaflet, a photograph and a lease are not — and he accepted the description and said he has no answer to it and has had eleven weeks.** Then the argument, which is better than the terms: the wall's best sentence has no force, a rule against being bought is worth nothing to a room that must choose between heat and being bought, in about four years one of them will need a wage, the person who finds the wage will own the wall without stealing it and without meaning to, and a thing paid for is a thing expected, which he has watched four times and paid for twice. **Nobody answered it. A man of twenty-two said the thing that made it worse: he is the only person in this city who can be telephoned about that room, and if his signature moves the money then the first action in that company when the money stops is to ring him, and he would then be the person deciding whether a room off a service road is a room, and he has been telling that room since a Monday three weeks ago that nobody in it can be asked whether he is right.** A woman of twenty-four warned once that clause three is always circumvented by a route that is not a lie, that she has watched about nine of them and none of those nine was a villain and two are people she would have sworn to, and that her own has not been asked for and will not be described. Nobody thanked anybody and the sheet lay face up on the counter for the first time in its history. A woman of about thirty was in that room for all nine minutes and the man of forty-one was told nothing whatever about her.
5. **Ch 235, the Friday of week 80 — what the room can do with it, which is nothing.** Ten jobs, nine in date, and a page on a job sheet that came up twice in one week: a stair fire door whose closer was adjusted in the spring and again midweek, reported twice in a week by two different people into the same box, both accounts true and neither the fault, the fault being about two millimetres of swell on the hinge side of a frame stamped with a number about four feet up. He reset the closer, wrote in the description box that the fault is not the closer, and a district man of thirty-eight said it was the first entry in about four months that had stated what the fault was not. **A cook of twenty-seven gave the five parts of what that room can do with a page of paper, unasked: it cannot take it, because four names stand on the lease and a fifth on a company's paper is a name that can be looked up; it cannot decline it, because declining is a letter and a letter is a document about the room bearing its occupants' names, which a man of fifty-eight established in that room three weeks earlier; it cannot give it away, because there is nobody to give it to, a man of fifty-one with a tool bag having told them himself that he can get into about nine hundred buildings and cannot get to one individual; neither the binder nor the board takes it, each being barred by one of the wall's own two lines, neither of which was read aloud; and fifth, the only available act is to leave it visible on a counter and remove it when the shutter comes down — which is the sentence she gave about a pair of shoes about four weeks earlier, and she announced that she has now said it twice and will not say it a third time and that whoever wrote down either occasion had better be a different person.** **Asha Reed said the consequence out loud: the room will have to decline a page of paper, no declining of a page of paper is other than a paper, that is the first object in about twelve weeks the room has had no way of holding, she had considered adding a twelfth line to the wall about money for two days and had not, and she wanted that said before anybody assumed she had forgotten — because a line about money written by a person frightened about money is the worst thing that board could carry, and a man of fifty-eight demonstrated the shape of that three weeks ago over a pair of shoes, and she is transferring it to a company.** A man of thirty-four who works nights said his two sentences a second time, having announced the previous Friday that he would, and added that he is not going to ask anybody to act, having asked in a corridor about four hundred times with no result and no form available, and that this is the fourth room in which he has said it and the first in which he was allowed to finish. A cook of twenty-seven told him it was not nothing; he replied that it is not nothing and is not a yes, that he has been able to tell the two apart since about the spring, and that he had said so to nobody until that night. **A man of twenty-two said one sentence: that there has been no money in that room since the year began, that about eleven people a week in this city are still going to be taught that a release waits for a man in a lanyard, that the two propositions occupied one building that evening, and that nothing in the room relates them and nothing in the city does — and nobody asked him what he meant and he did not say it again, and about four people in that room returned to it afterwards and about four understood it and about four did not.**
6. **Ch 236, the Monday of week 81 — eleven lines, nine arguments, and one thumb.** **The room was ninety-one days old, which is a quarter of a year, and a first-year of nineteen said so out loud and a cook of twenty-seven confirmed the figure, said she had counted twice because she counts things, and declined to say what it means on the ground that she does not know and will not make a coincidence out of it in front of nine people; the number went on a scrap into a pocket and is not reproduced anywhere.** A cook of twenty-seven brought one sheet off a roll in a kitchen, distinguished it from the wall and from the ring binder, and a first-year of nineteen stated the volume's own definition before a mark was made: **a rule in this building is a line somebody wrote by hand; it costs its writer something said aloud; it binds nobody who did not write it; its own writer may lift it with a thumb; and it is evidence of nothing in any proceeding, there being none.** They wrote for about an hour and forty minutes and about nine of the eleven lines were argued about. **A man of fifty-eight who inspects buses, who is not the man of fifty-eight with the bad hip and was named as not being him out loud while he fetched his pen, wrote that a thing which has failed twice is not a thing anybody should be committed to paper, and said the cost of that sentence out loud as twenty-two years of about nine vehicles a week, and said he has the standing to say a thing is not safe and has never once had it in a room with a door in it.** A printer of fifty-eight wrote twice, argued that a board cannot be lifted and a drawer can and that in about four years somebody will find it, and conceded that this is not a reason but a thing she wants. A cook of twenty-seven wrote a second line at about half past eight and rubbed it off with her own thumb at about a quarter to nine in front of about nine people, on the ground that it came out of annoyance, that such a line will be wrong by Friday, and that a line which can be lifted should be lifted by the person who laid it down — and said aloud that she did it herself, that nobody helped, and that nobody had touched that paper all evening except to write on it. **A woman of thirty-one with a key and a diary wrote the eleventh line last, after the sheet had gone round, and signed it separately beneath the other names: that she may be telephoned about any of it and will come and reverse it. It is the same eleven words she put on a sheet of typing paper about a fortnight earlier, and she named her own cost unasked: that one was about a key and this one is about eleven lines and about a person in the third chair who was not asked, and that she wants it in the same hand and wants it to be the one anybody rings.** Eight names are at the foot. **A man of twenty-two signed and wrote none of the eleven, as he signed the board and wrote none of its twelve, and gave the reason once, unasked, in about nine seconds: that no sentence he writes about that room avoids being one with his name across the front of it; that one such sentence has been in a rail in Lattice Ward for about a term and cost him the last thing he had; and that a first-year of nineteen should notice nobody argued. She said nobody had.** And a woman of about thirty was in that room for the whole afternoon and the sheet never reached her, the reason announced out loud by the first-year and handed back by the woman in her own words and not improved on: nobody has put a question to her in about twelve weeks, nobody is going to begin on a Monday afternoon holding paper, she would like to be left in the chair and not made into the thing a person is careful about, and nobody said that for her. Nobody did. **A first-year of nineteen stated, before anybody else could, that the sheet is not a constitution and is not to be called a set of rules, and that anybody describing it as either in four years is describing nine people sitting in a room on a Monday afternoon.** At about nine a man of twenty-two said the one thing about what it is worth, and nobody thanked him for it: there is no way in this city to make anybody obey any of those eleven lines and there never will be, about four of them are already wrong, a woman of fifty-eight has written the reason down twice now, and the reason a man of fifty-eight in this city could be within the law for four years and still have done what he did is on the sixth line of that sheet in a hand that is not his.
7. **Ch 237, the Thursday of week 81 — what this room has got wrong.** The hardboard came off its two nails and went on a chair, and about nine people looked at the bare wall behind it, and a first-year of nineteen set the terms before anybody sat down: the failures lead, because a record of what a room does right is a document about a room, and about nine of the eleven people present had never been in a room that would say aloud what it had got wrong, and she had been in about nine such rooms and had not known that was the missing thing until somebody said it with witnesses watching. **She went first and said so, and it was the four seconds in the week of seventy-two on a Tuesday to a woman she had never met who had telephoned the plate, with six people in the room and no request to any of them; she said everybody had known for about a fortnight and nobody had made her say it, that she had carried the four seconds for about seven weeks, and that saying it aloud was the whole of what she had to give. Her own price, unasked: about eleven hundred leaflets in about eleven hundred pockets in a market with her name in her own hand and no mechanism in this city for removing a name from paper; that she put it there deliberately after being told twice that a description of a room is a description of the people in it; that she is the only nineteen-year-old in that room; and that about four people there have wanted to ask her something for about two months.** **A printer of fifty-eight read nine words of her own out loud in that room for the first time in her life**, on a clean sheet in the binder, about a proof and a line that will be read wrong, written in that room in the first fortnight in her own hand in about nine seconds and never since spoken aloud in a room. She set the rest out first: about nine hundred leaves of a company's pack carrying her handwriting at nine inches, about four hundred readers, her certainty that a printed page cannot be un-printed after being wrong four times in forty years and right once, that the once has cost her sleep and nothing else because nobody telephoned the shop and the pack bears no number of hers, and that her delay was not modesty but not knowing whether that room would let a woman of fifty-eight hear her own handwriting read by herself. A cook of twenty-seven read eleven items, the first nine and the tenth hers, and the eleventh a woman's name struck through in one unretraced stroke with a biro in about a second and a half in front of about nine witnesses, none of whom stopped it or was asked to; **she declined to name the woman and said so before being asked twice**, and gave the whole of it: a courtesy extended to a relative on a line about her own pot, a request by telephone from work, a mark that cannot be lifted because a finger lifts ink and leaves paper, a future reader finding a ghost and no indication that any request was made, and a smudge as the only surviving record — and then that she was told at the time she was not the person holding the pen, that this was correct, that she has turned it over daily for a fortnight, and that it stands on the board in a first-year's hand as the only sentence in that room ever to have cost its writer something. **A woman of thirty-one with a key and a diary read hers: a cut key on a ring with a label worn to a shape, eleven words on a sheet of typing paper, and a clean sheet written from a telephone call by a woman in another district about a man who was in the room, being the only item in that binder no one there can withdraw, existing because a cook of twenty-seven said to proceed and a first-year of nineteen announced aloud that she was about to do the thing the rule in the other room forbids. Her price, unasked: a rule given away and then written down again in a kitchen, and a second thing she is findable about.** A man of twenty-nine on an employer's Friday list read nine words, a use rather than a practice, the shortest item in the binder, the only one naming a building, about four readers, an employer unaware; price: about four people. **A man of fifty-eight with a bad hip read his: that he brought nine steps belonging to his sister on a Thursday afternoon, that the room declined them, that the refusal was correct, and that the line he went on to write on the board about a thing whose words are somebody else's arrived about three weeks after the afternoon in question and is about three weeks late — and he corrected his own earlier statement in that room that a pair of shoes was the first thing the room was able to hand back, saying the first was the afternoon. Nobody told him he was wrong.** The man with the docket read nothing and was given nothing, and the entry records the absence as a fact and not as a virtue. **A woman of twenty-four requested that her three sentences in that room over about three weeks not be repeated, one of them being that saying a thing badly is the only available shape since doing it well produces a question about method, there being no method, and the method being a person, and the person being findable; that she has never been in a photograph of that room, that this is neither modesty nor a rule, that it is the only arrangement in that building she has been able to rely on, and that she is neither breaking it nor keeping it for nothing. The room agreed in about four seconds. Her own account of the cost: agreement alters nothing, about eleven people heard her on a Thursday night, and nothing in this city removes a sentence from a person, which is the line said eleven times in a corridor seventeen days earlier, twice wrongly and unrepaired. Nobody thanked her, nobody wrote it down and nobody repeated it.** **Then a man of fifty-one with a tool bag said the thing that made the evening a decision, to the floor: that about eleven individuals had just been spoken aloud inside a building bearing a company's number on its outer wall; that he has spent about nine years setting numbers on about nine hundred doors in four buildings and has never been able to learn who passes one and has wanted to for about nine years, a plate being a service and nobody being owed a list; that the room accomplished in an evening what he has wanted for nine years without anybody in it making a mistake; and that he will not be thanked for observing it and will not be the one to say what it is for. Being asked by a first-year of nineteen to say what it is for, he said that an organisation able to establish that a room exists may enter that room in a list, that about eleven people a week in this city are already entered in a list in the same building, and that the two lists differ while their membership does not. Nothing further was said about it that night and no chapter of this movement treats it as settled.** The wall was then read line by line, twelve lines, each by its author, in about four minutes; the first line is still the first line and is a cook's; the board went back on its nails at about ten and the binder was shut and not reopened.
8. **Ch 238, the Monday of week 82 — the same eleven, and not the same list.** Seven people at about ten past ten and about eleven by eleven, the difference being the kettle and the hours people keep, and a district nurse of thirty-four sat beside the counter with a clipboard, drank one cup of tea, asked nothing, and is thanked by nobody then or on any of her visits. **A letting clerk of thirty-nine took a call at about eleven. On the Friday a man from her own floor rang, asked whether the unit was still vacant, received the standing answer she has now given five times, and then said the unit is not vacant and that he had been so informed by somebody who had read it out of a record; he could give the street and the day and could not give the name of one person in the room; she informed him that this was the correct state of affairs and that it was hers, being the one who tells him. Her one sentence: she is not thanked, will not be, keeps a sheet bearing four calls, and is still not bringing it there. Nobody raised the sheet; it has been raised three times.** A person of thirty-four who buys training, no standing and none claimed, owed nothing and having said so twice, put the useful half first: on the Friday she was asked, by somebody in her own building, to enter that room in a list of places, not a document but a board in a corridor, the manner in which a café is entered; she declined; her reason for declining is not regard for the room but that a company unable to establish that the room exists cannot enter it on anything, and that on Thursday night, in a room holding about eleven people, the room became establishable, and that she was not present and was informed afterwards by about four people who were. She then gave the half concerning her own building: about eleven persons a week stand on a list there, they have done for years, it is a list of people sent to things and not of places, about eleven a week attend, nobody at that end has ever been in that room, nobody in that room has ever stood on that list, she has examined it and never seen a name from that room on it, and had never seen the room on it before the Friday. Asked whether that constitutes a price, she said it does not, and that the price is that the room is now discoverable by persons able to act, having formerly been discoverable only through a number on a plate and a bell-push that does not ring by itself; she declined to call that bad news standing in that room, and said a first-year of nineteen named the price of a findable room to her about six weeks ago and was right. **A man of fifty-one with a tool bag then said that he stands on that list, has for about nine years, that it is a rota carrying a box that reads that no reason is given by the holder, that he entered his own name into it about four years ago, that it is still there, that nobody has ever questioned him and he has never wanted to be questioned; asked whether the name could be removed, he said it could be done in about four seconds with a telephone, that he has not done it, does not intend to, has been considering it for about nine years, has done nothing, and will not pretend otherwise; and that about eleven persons a week stand on it across this city and he has never once examined the other ten.** Unasked, he said that the man with the docket stands on it also, for about two years, on an exception recorded and renewed for the term, in the same box, and that he knows because he has processed four renewals that were not requested and for which no reason was asked; that a man in that room therefore stands twice over, on a company's roll and on a rail in another district, has stood twice over for about a term, that neither knew of the other, that this is the whole of what he came to say, and that he will not be thanked and will not be questioned further. Nobody thanked him. Nobody looked at the man with the docket, who had said nothing for about an hour. **A first-year of nineteen then said once that the eleven are the same eleven and that the list is not the same list; asked for her grounds she said she has none she can prove, that she has said it regardless, and that a man of fifty-eight established about a fortnight earlier that a line incapable of proof is still worth having, and that this is the only authority she has and she is spending it about a room rather than about an object in a plastic bag, and that she would like that observed. Nobody improved on it and the authority is entered as borrowed.** Two correct acts: the door, the sounder and the outside of the shutter at about half past ten by a woman of thirty-one with a key and a diary, unaided, unasked and unobserved, the shutter identical on the Tuesday morning; and a man of thirty-four who works nights arriving at about half past eleven on his way to a shift and asking what the heat in that room costs to run, about four of those present unable to say, this being the first occasion in fourteen weeks on which the price of the heat and the price of anything else have shared a sentence in that room, about nine people hearing the question, nobody answering it, and him leaving for work. Neither act altered anything.
9. **Ch 239, the Thursday of week 82 — a key that comes back.** The room was a hundred and one days old, the company's page had been face up on the counter for a fortnight, and there were about eleven people in it at half past eight. The man of twenty-two had no docket, which nobody remarked on and about nine people noticed. **He telephoned a woman of thirty-one at about half past eight, was put through by the clerk of thirty-nine after the standing sentence and a fourth *hold on*, and told her it was about the key; she took the call with her diary open, declined to close it, recited the eleven words from the sheet, and observed that a man telephoning on a Thursday evening is telephoning about a key or about the heat; he said key; she said she would be there in about an hour and would bring nothing, and arrived at about half past nine, and was thanked by nobody and appeared to expect it.** He put a cut key on the counter and said the return is neither a protest nor connected to the money, said first so that nobody need rearrange a face afterwards. **Then the argument, in order: that a key is worth exactly as much as a telephone, which is the sentence on that leaf of typing paper; that he had let it sit eleven weeks without acting; and that this is the whole of what he has done wrong. That one key is unreachable because its counterparty is an organisation; that a first-year of nineteen reduced the question to a scrap on the back of a docket about eleven weeks earlier; that four people answered it; that nobody wrote the answers down; and that the answer for the unreachable key is still nobody. That eleven weeks of two keys in a single pocket is how a list starts, because a person eventually records which pocket; that in about four years a man with two keys in one pocket, in a city where nobody knows all nine, is a post; that the entire argument of that room about keys is that the giver can take them back; and that he has been holding the key that makes the argument false beside the key that makes it true and would rather hold the second.** Then, to the room and not to the woman of thirty-one: that nothing done wrong occurred in fourteen weeks; that the eleven words on a leaf of typing paper are the best thing anybody has ever done for that room; that the key is not being handed back to her but because of what a key is; that she is not the problem, that the woman with a ring file is not the problem, and that he is not the problem; and that he would like it on paper and knows it cannot go on paper, and has therefore said it to about nine people. **A cook of twenty-seven, from a sink, said one word that was neither assent nor refusal and gave its purpose: he has made himself less able to open that door; he did it aloud in front of about eleven people; nobody there will call that a good thing; she will not; and she does not believe he did it to be good. He agreed he could not carry the key and could not put the reason in fewer words. The woman of thirty-one lifted it at about ten to ten, looked at the label, put it in her pocket, and said she will not draw up a list, will not write it down, will not say she holds it; that he rings her if he wants it again; that a ninth holder would have to be the ninth; and that she will not explain any of that to about eleven people, having explained it once, on a scrap in a folder on a shelf in a room she has never entered. A man of thirty-four who works nights asked what the scrap says, was told it is a question one of them wrote on the back of a docket, and answered that the best sentence in the room is a question, that he has wanted somebody to telephone him for about two terms, that he has just watched a key handed back instead, and that he declines to say which of the two is larger.** He then said he would decline the paper on the counter at about half past seven on the Friday in about four minutes, that there is nothing on it he can answer, that the man who read it aloud was right about the single thing he was right about, and that he has not slept properly about it. **A cook of twenty-seven then said the heating goes off on the Monday, that nobody in that room has ever established what it costs to run, that a man had put that question to her the previous morning and she had no answer, that she is not blaming him and will not permit anyone else to, and that she would like it written down and it cannot be written down. He agreed that it cannot, called that the correct answer, and said he has nothing better.** The key that stayed is untouched, unreachable, raised by nobody that evening except its holder; no holder was invented, no lock fitted, no list made and no mechanism proposed, and the chapter settles nothing about it. A woman of about thirty sat near the door for the whole evening and was not asked for her view of it, which is the correct answer and the whole of what that room has ever put at her disposal.
10. **Ch 240, the Friday of week 82 — the close: a refusal, a fortnight, and eight words over a door.** Nine jobs and nine in date, a bin-store timer that is not the fault among them, and a stair fire door whose closer he adjusted on the Wednesday, again on the Friday and again that evening, which came up a third time with a stamped identifier in the description box that he did not write, copied by a district man of thirty-eight off the frame at about four feet with a step-ladder; he went and looked, the frame is as he wrote it, he reset the closer a third time, wrote the word again, expects it on the Monday, and will not be the person anybody approaches. **The refusal took about four minutes and about nine people spoke in it.** A first-year of nineteen stated it before anybody else could compose a face: the answer is no; not because the terms are poor, the reader having said before reading them that they were the best anybody had put to that room; and not because the money is unwanted, a man of thirty-seven having said on the Monday of the previous week that there is no money in that room and being right. Asked why, she said nothing on that page can be answered by anyone in it, that she has been there daily for fourteen weeks awaiting an answer that has not come, and that she was not seeking one. **A man of twenty-two added two halves: that he declined to treat the page as a question about the offer, which is what he had been doing since the previous Friday, and that a first-year told him he had been waiting to be asked his opinion and had not asked, and that the two are one thing; and that he did not vote, that about nine of those present can be relieved of a thing by telephone inside a day, that he cannot be relieved of that room at all and was not relieved of it that evening, and that he would like it recorded and has nowhere to record it.** A cook of twenty-seven set the kettle going at its fixed point and then, without stopping work and without being asked, said she would not be thanked, would not be argued with, and would give one figure and then wash up. **The figure is a fortnight. The heating goes off on the Monday; nobody has paid for it; nobody is going to; she does not have the money and it is not hers to find; and she is not going to stand in a room on a Friday night and pass off a kettle as a radiator.** Asked whether it could be found, she said no, and gave her ground: the only route by which money is found for a room of this kind is a person who wants something; every version of that has been refused for about fourteen weeks; the heating is what the refusing costs; and she has been setting that kettle going daily for about fourteen weeks while pretending in about nine separate ways that she had not noticed. **A man of thirty-seven collected the page at about eight, in his own coat, without sitting down and without apology: he had been shown right on the Monday about a matter he had not been discussing; his file will record the offer as declined; he is forbidden to record the reason and will not; and he has been given an instruction concerning his own handwriting and has complied, and this constitutes his entire contribution to the evening. Nobody thanked him then and nobody thanked him on the Monday. The counter has carried nothing since.** **A man of about forty-five at the doorway, who had not been in that room before, said he had read about it on a sheet circulating in about eleven hundred pockets, and put one question: whether it will still be there. The man of twenty-two announced beforehand that he has said nine words to somebody from off that room three times in this case, that he is not improving on the other three, and that he says so first so that nobody may afterwards treat it as a sentence. The answer, nine words, counted on the page: nobody knows who will be here in a fortnight.** The questioner said it was a no. He was told it is not a no, that it is nine words and the truth, that the truth is not a no, and that he has been trying for three weeks to do better. The man left; nobody asked his name, nobody wrote him down, and no record exists anywhere in this city that he stood in that doorway. Then somebody looked at the board over the door, which has carried four words in a sign-writer's hand since the second week of the room, and **above them, in another hand and another ink, eight words: that it is warmer in here than out there. A first-year of nineteen stated that nobody had seen them. A cook of twenty-seven, at a sink, without turning round, said she wrote them on the Thursday night after the room emptied and returned on the Friday morning; that she is not going to be told off about it; that a line written by hand costs its writer something and binds nobody who did not write it; and that she is not going to say what it cost her tonight. Asked outright, she said the room is warm on a Friday because she has set that kettle going nightly for about fourteen weeks, that a kettle does not heat, that it has not heated since the second week, and that about nine people in that room have known it throughout.** **The final sentence of the evening was spoken by a first-year of nineteen, standing, in about nine seconds, to about nine people: that the board over the door is not the wall and the two are not a single object; that the first line on the hardboard remains the first line on the hardboard, is a cook's, concerns what the room will not keep, is not a rule, binds nobody, and is evidence of nothing in any proceeding; that the eight words above the door are not a rule either; and that from the Monday they will be the only line in that room that is untrue, and that she would like everybody to look at it while it is. Nobody said anything after it.** **The fortnight of heating is not paid for by any person in this volume and is not resolved anywhere in it; no fund, collection, subscription, grant, list, minute or appeal exists and none was proposed by anybody in that room; and the cost falls on the people who come because the room is warm, and among them on a woman of about thirty who was in a chair near the door for the whole of the evening with a bag on her lap, who has attended on every working day since that Friday in week sixty-nine, who has been asked nothing by anybody in fourteen weeks, who is on no object in that room and is in no file in this case, who was not thanked, who is owed nothing by anybody, and to whom nobody apologised, which was the correct course and is the whole of what this movement cost. The man who said no does not pay it and is owed nothing by anybody. The entry does not pay it, does not resolve it, and proposes no mechanism for it.**

## What Movement IV of Volume 05 did not do, and what it leaves for Movement V

**It did not teach, demonstrate, assess or assist anything; nobody was shown anything; and the condition is quoted in none of the ten chapters and paraphrased in none of them, and a room off a service road is not a practice in that department and the reason is not that he is the exception.** It did not stage the relay, ask the woman who carried it, or name her, and the word *carrier* does not appear in any of the ten files. It put no second panel and no `* * *` marker in the volume. It did not put a name over the door, and it put no title, office, post, committee, minute, notice, report, form, registration or constitution on anybody, and the only documents this movement produced are a company's page in a company's own hand and a sheet of kitchen paper with eleven lines in it. **It did not let anything be settled by any of them: the offer was refused in about four minutes in a room and minuted nowhere, the rules were written by hand on a sheet of kitchen paper and one of them was rubbed off with a thumb by the person who wrote it, and the records were read aloud and not written down.** It did not open the Exchange book anywhere but Chapter 233, the book is at forty-five lines, and **nobody signed anything in any chapter of this movement, which is the first movement in the volume in which nobody signed anything.** It did not resolve the standing problem, invented no rule about who may be asked, and attached no number to one. **It handed a key back and it did not fix the first key, and it created no mechanism, no holder and no list.** It did not empty the box in pencil and did not touch it. It did not let the master leave the fourth pocket of the tool roll and nobody decided anything about its fifth line. It did not name the licensor, name the person who inserted the page, name the clause behind the public version, put Iona or Sorn in any file, or let any message arrive from anybody. It did not put Rafi Pell in a room, name Steve Akers, find the woman of thirty-four with a hip, or say Asha Reed's retired sentence. **It gave the man of forty-one his offer and no second fact, and did not tell him about the photograph, the pack, the nine hundred or the woman of thirty, and he says nothing about any of them because none of them is mentioned to him.** It did not let a photograph become a copy. **It did not pay for the heating, did not resolve it, did not propose a mechanism for it, and said so in terms in the last entry of the movement.** And it did not resolve the relationship: there is no scene, no declaration and no kiss, the name Talia Venn appears in none of the ten files, a woman of twenty-four is in two of them and speaks once in each and never to him, and the two of them are in the same building in one chapter of this batch and do not speak.

---

## What Chapters 221–230 added to the record, in the order it is established

1. **Ch 221, the Friday of week 75 — eight printed pages with a photograph of that room in them, and a first-year who said yes in four seconds.** Eleven jobs, eight in date, a bin store where a man of thirty-eight has told three callers in a month they were right, the word from nine to ten at about one, and nobody in that yard asking where a release would come from. **A district nurse of thirty-four with a district jacket and a clipboard, who put a trestle in a market about five weeks earlier and has not been thanked for that either, laid a certificate prospectus on the counter: about four hundred readers in another city, and on the inside cover a photograph taken from that room's doorway with the shutter up, the nine chairs, the window and the wall about four feet behind it, the hardboard with four words in a sign-writer's hand, a piece of sandpaper, the ring binder standing open with its pages above the sleeve, and about six unresolvable figures behind the counter.** Asha Reed said two things and then the third: **that she said yes on a Tuesday about three weeks earlier in about four seconds to a woman who had telephoned the number on the plate because she had taken one of about eleven hundred leaflets out of her bag in a market, and that six people were standing in that room when the picture was taken and she asked not one of them.** She named the cost of her own two-year sentence — that nobody in a room has to say anything to be described by the person who has the room — and said she has said it on a floor in week sixty-eight and at a market table in week seventy and has not been careful with it. A cook of twenty-seven said one word from a sink. **A man of twenty-two said the photograph is not a room described by somebody who was not in it, and was told in front of about nine people that it is a room described by a person who was in it and did not ask six other people, which is worse and is a different thing.**
2. **Ch 222, the Wednesday of week 76 — the eighth sitting, the book shut, and a print run found in a shop four feet wide.** Eight sittings, seven gaps of four weeks, the tin into a drawer with no lock on it, the rule given whole, and the telephone count moved from thirty-three to thirty-five and announced unasked; she has now said it thirteen times, eight of which are the eight Wednesdays this book holds, and she performed that subtraction aloud, refused to repeat it, has not rung the caller who is not coming, and has refused her reason a third time in the same words. **The book did not open, and the reason is new: at the seventh sitting it grew, and since then the question asked of her most often is when it will grow again, and she cannot answer it, and a woman who cannot answer the only question there is does not open a book on a table in front of about nine people.** Two items at four minutes each, neither written down; a man of twenty-two in a corner for two hours, not thanked. **Then four feet of shop: a printer of fifty-eight and a clerk of forty-four, neither in the room and neither thanked, and a page with a photograph on it, and about nine hundred leaflets off one counter in one morning carrying the same picture and the words *our method* beneath it — and the top sheet in that sleeve legible, and the hand on it the printer's own nine words.**
3. **Ch 223, the Thursday of week 76 — nine steps that are a woman's, a refusal, and a telephone call to a plate.** A man of fifty-eight with a hip that did not go right, a bus and one stop too far, and nine steps for getting out of the front seat of a car when one hip will not take weight, written by his sister of sixty-four on the back of an envelope in a car park about four years ago because he asked her to. **He said before anybody asked that the words are not his, that he did not learn them, and that the difference between performing a thing and holding it is the whole of that room.** A first-year of nineteen refused, without walking to the wall, because the only person who could take a page back is a woman in another district who has not been asked — **and said the offer was right and the refusal was right in the same breath.** He named two failures, the second on a pavement outside a shop in front of about nine people, and said the sequence therefore qualifies as a technique by that room's own definition, which he can still remember from the wall in its first week. He named the price: four years in a drawer, and a sister he has not spoken to in about nine years. **The room then did nothing for four days, which is the instrument it has; and at about seven that evening the sister telephoned the number on a plate with a company's name on it, a letting clerk of thirty-nine gave her the sentence about a vacant frontage, and the clerk said hold on.** The sister said the words herself, faster, with a different word for the count, adding two things he had not said: that the chair is not optional, and that where another person is helping the count is not taken by the person being helped. **The page went into the binder as the seventh thing; the first-year announced out loud that she was about to break the other room's rule, was told to go ahead, and gave her whole justification in about nine words — she said it to a person, and not to a tin — and added that she cannot prove the difference and has decided it.** He read it, found a number in it he has asked her for about two hundred times in nine years and never heard, and said she was wrong, and was told that that is also correct.
4. **Ch 224, the Monday of week 77 — a room that is open, and what the openness cost.** A man of thirty-one in the fourth week of a stoppage over the removal of a four-minute torch walk-round of the rear of a loaded vehicle, at a depot of about ninety vehicles and about four hundred loaders; not a supervisor, has never wanted to be, and said twice unprompted that it is not about money. **Nobody asked which side he was on, nobody asked whether the check should be reinstated, no line of the wall was read at him, and about four people watched a first-year of nineteen decide in front of everybody not to reach for the wall.** A woman of thirty-one with a key and a diary did the door, the sounder and the outside of the shutter with nine people present where she expects four, unaided, and the shutter was the same in the morning. He asked her one question; they talked for about two hours; she walked him to a stop that does not come to a service road. **At about four o'clock, at the sink, she said she was going to have to stop: one of her four employers draws about nine people a week from an agency, that depot is on the same agency, a woman with a clipboard asked her in a corridor about three weeks ago whether she was friendly with anybody on strike, she said no and it was true, and there is nothing in writing anywhere, which is the whole difficulty and the whole of why it works.** Nineteen, nine Fridays a week across four employers, about ninety pounds, about two hundred in the account. **A man of twenty-two said the correct thing about the room and was told it was not enough: the silence is right and is not a kindness, and being the one who has to be the friend is a job nobody there is paid for and she is the only person who cannot afford it.**
5. **Ch 225, the Thursday of week 78 — THE MIDPOINT. Four lines in a company's typeface that use no pronoun at all, and the two words *our method* set above them.** A pack of about forty pages, page two carrying the photograph, and under it: a working method, in a real room; the room asks a person what they do and records it in their own words; nothing is claimed, nothing is standardised, and the practitioner can withdraw at any time. **The printer of fifty-eight read them aloud twice from the counter, unasked, and then finished a sentence the man of twenty-two had started and could not finish, which is the finding: ten lines on a hardboard in six hands — the first a cook's about withdrawal, the second a first-year's about claims of improvement — printed in a company's voice under a company's possessive on page two of a pack with about nine hundred impressions of a leaflet off one counter in one morning.** The clerk of forty-four said the other half, including that she took the pack off an open shelf without leaving a note and chose in about four seconds to be the askable party. **A man of fifty-one with a tool bag, on the record about about nine hundred wrong numbers on about nine hundred doors and unable to change one, said a plate is a service item inside a building and a leaflet is not, and that recovery would need about nine hundred visits to a service road that four buses a day serve, and that he has not been thanked and is not going to be.** **A first-year of nineteen stood up and said she was not taking the leaflet down — no mechanism exists, and a public withdrawal would make her the only person anybody could consult about how she did it — and then said out loud that she agreed to the photograph in week seventy-two in about four seconds, that six people were in the room, that she asked none of them, and that she does not know whether the six in the picture are the six in that room; and was told that she does not, and that this is worse.** A nurse of twenty-four said that a person can agree to a photograph of a room and be in the photograph. **The room decided in about four minutes not to write to the company, because a letter from four signatories in a building with a company's number on the outside is a document about that room; nothing was sent, drafted or minuted.**
6. **Ch 226, the Friday of week 78 — a company asks the right question and the answer is that five of six cannot be found.** Eleven jobs, nine in date, and an emergency lighting pack that passed its test with cells two years out of date and was replaced anyway, which is now the reason that stair will never be examined again. **A call to a company's number on a plate with no name on it, answered by a letting clerk of thirty-nine who said hold on; about nine minutes in all, none of it anybody's fault.** A woman asked who was in the photograph and whether they had agreed to it, and a first-year of nineteen counted for four minutes and got six: a cook of twenty-seven, herself, a printer of fifty-eight, a man of twenty-nine on an employer's Friday list, a woman of thirty-one with a key and a diary, and a man of twenty-two. **The six figures in the picture are those six and the count closes, and five of the six cannot be located by anybody outside that room and the one who can be located is the man with the docket; the seventh person in that room, a woman of about thirty with a bag on her lap, is not in the picture and is the only one of the seven whose face is not in it, and nobody asked her and about four people wanted to.** The caller withdrew the pack from sale, from the next printing and from the course, and the room said why that was worth nothing before she could, and she said her own part and asked for it to be attributed to her and not to a room. **A woman of twenty-six who had taken a leaflet off a market table in week seventy asked whether what people say in that room stays in that room, and got nine words counted on the page — *That photograph is not of a room any more* — and he said out loud first that it is the third such answer and is not an improvement on the two before it.** The clerk of thirty-nine came back, said this is the third time in about two months the plate has been how somebody found that room, that she holds a sheet with all three calls on it, that it is hers and she is not bringing it here, was asked for it, and refused in one word.
7. **Ch 227, the Monday of week 79 — a piece of hardboard bought for two pounds, a corridor, eleven repetitions, and nine Fridays.** **A first-year of nineteen stood eleven feet from a desk in a ground-floor clinic corridor in Lattice Ward, where about nine people an hour hand something to a stranger, and said the first line of that room's wall eleven times, and wrote one line and not the other nine, giving the reason standing up: the other nine are about what a room will not do and this one is about what it will not keep, and a person about to hand something over at a desk needs the second kind.** Nine of about eleven stopped. **It went wrong twice and she corrected neither — once by saying it too fast, and once when a man of about sixty returned it with the sense inverted, and she refused to correct him because correcting a stranger creates a person holding a sentence in the wrong order with no means of finding out, and no form anywhere takes a sentence off a person. At about eleven a woman wrote to the clinic about a girl shouting in a corridor, the letter went on a board by the desk where that clinic displays anything anybody has written, a woman behind the desk read it out to about four people in a queue, nobody rang the first-year, and one of her four employers — a Friday at that clinic — was covered by somebody else at about eleven that morning. About ninety pounds out of an account of about two hundred.** A man of twenty-two said the thing would be on a wall within a month and photographed, and was told he was about eleven miles away on a docket; he then said she should have asked somebody first, and was told that there is nobody to ask and that this is the entire content of the wall the board was bought for. **And she named the sentence she did not invent: a nurse of twenty-four, whom she has never spoken to, said in a corridor outside a clinic a few months back that a clinic asks a person for nine things at a desk before it asks one question about any of it — and that both sentences are true, and that the nurse's is the one that would get a person arrested and hers is the one a person can act on at a desk.** The letter is not wrong and she put her there.
8. **Ch 228, the Tuesday of week 79 — a pair of shoes, and the first thing the room can hand straight back.** **A man of fifty-eight with the same bad hip came back nineteen days after he was refused, said he had not brought the envelope, and put a pair of shoes on the counter: resoled eleven times on the left and not on the right, cut by hand because a machine does not do that shape, wearable for about an hour and a half.** He said it was not a practice, not a method, not his sister's and not his; that a man who cuts soles by hand had asked him a question about the heel, the answer was his own and is on the back of a receipt; and that he would like to leave them where somebody could see them, the reason being that he wanted to be able to pick them up and put them down again without asking anybody. **Told they can stay until the shutter comes down and then he takes them home, which is neither a favour nor a rule and is the only thing in that building that has ever worked.** Then the wall, line by line: a thing liftable in about nine seconds satisfies the first line and cannot satisfy a binder or a page, and one page has been struck through once and cannot be restored; a shoe admits of no copy; a shoe has never been printed; the tenth line was not invoked. **A cook of twenty-seven said a kettle, a sheet of cloth and a pair of shoes are none of them a technique, and a record of any of them would in about four years put a card in front of somebody saying they had failed, and in this case the card would be about a man's feet — but an object may stay on a counter where it is visible and be taken away at the end of a day, and that is a different class of event from a page; and she refused to say it twice.** He read his sister's page, found a number in it he has asked her for about two hundred times in nine years and never heard, and established that it exists because a woman telephoned a company's number and was written down by somebody who has never met her, and said she was wrong. **At the door he said four words to a man of twenty-two who had said nothing all afternoon: that this was the first time in eleven weeks that somebody had brought him something he could hand back in a minute. He was told the shoes were handable because of what he had done and not because of the room.**
9. **Ch 229, the Wednesday of week 79 — who held the camera, and why the hand is moving.** Four feet of shop in the Quiet Quarter, about four hundred leaflets left out of nine hundred, a quarterly return on a card of the shop's own, and a hundred and forty-one pounds under a till in an envelope with no name on it that cannot be sent back and has not moved since the spring. **A person of forty-one with a first floor in Nine Bridges and a van, a customer of that counter for about four years, said they had known since the Wednesday and did not pretend otherwise, and then said the thing nobody in that shop had understood: a binder open on a counter with a sheet proud of the sleeve reads in a still exposure as a certificate, and at about a fortieth of a second the hand on it moved, and the movement is what reads as a room, and it was set deliberately in about nine seconds for that reason — so the legibility of six people's handwriting is a craft decision taken to stop the picture looking like a document. Their summary, said once, without heat: no conspiracy and no fault is involved and the whole of the accident is a shutter speed.** Four exposures in about eleven minutes; a brief reading *a working method, in a real room*, *with people in it if possible*, *not posed*; one paid for and three not. **A person of thirty-four who buys training, who wrote the brief, said she has never once asked to see a person in a photograph, gave the reason — the room is somebody else's, they would not identify their own occupants, and the exchange would have run about nine weeks and she had a course to write — and said the part she could not improve: a young woman answered a telephone in about four seconds and said yes, she wrote that down in her own book as a yes, and three colleagues that week used the same word about a supplier.** **The printer of fifty-eight said she has been wrong about printed matter four times in forty years and right once, and the once is her own handwriting nine inches tall on the top of page two, and that she is not a mechanism, has never asked to be on that wall, and that the name a reader arrives at is a shop with a telephone number on the outside of it, and no number on the pack is hers.** And the person of forty-one said they will not destroy the three uncommissioned photographs in a drawer, because a person paid for a job and the job was done, and because in about four years that drawer would be the only evidence in this city that anybody stood in that room who is not in the pack, and there is no mechanism for handing three photographs back.
10. **Ch 230, the Thursday of week 79 — the close: twelve lines in seven hands, seven things, and a corridor.** About eleven people, four standing; a first-year of nineteen stating for the third time that the room is busier and smaller at once. **A man of twenty-two stood in the floor rather than at the back for the first time in eleven weeks, unremarked and observed by about nine people.** The wall went to **twelve lines in seven hands and eight names**. **The eleventh line was written first, by the man of fifty-eight, standing up, and read back to the room before he let anybody know he wanted it there: that it will not keep a thing whose words are somebody else's — eleven words, the only thing he has learned in nineteen days, and he did not have it on the afternoon the room refused his sister's sequence. Asha Reed said out loud that he is the second man of fifty-eight to write in that room and not the first, that the first was the Monday of week seventy and refused to write anything, and that she thinks about him about twice a week.** The twelfth line was hers: that it will tell you what it will not hold before you hand it over. **She said the room had begun four days earlier to do this in a corridor in about ninety minutes with a board bought for about two pounds, had it go wrong twice, once in her mouth and once in a man's, and fixed neither, lost nine Fridays and about ninety pounds, and would do it again; that doing it again is worse than being told; and that both versions are on the table.** A man of twenty-nine who works on lifts asked what the corridor is for and was told it is for the people who are not here, and that it cannot be done every day, or on a Friday, or indoors. **A nurse of twenty-four gave the finding once: saying it badly is the only shape available, because doing it well would produce a question about how, and there is no how, and the how is a person, and the person is findable; and that she has never been in a photograph, that this is not modesty and not a rule, and that it is the only arrangement in that building she has been able to rely on.** The binder came off the shelf, was opened on the counter and not on the floor, and has **seven things**; the first-year read the number and the hands and the contents of none, as she has done once before, and said **six of the seven carry his name, that he is the only one of the seven anybody can go and find, and that he and she are both going to do nothing about it**, and that a man of fifty-one with a tool bag has said so to her privately. **A woman of thirty-one with a key and a diary found the sounder loose where a trolley had struck it, put two screws into it in about four minutes, and it rang and nothing happened, because a sound at a man on a step does not make anybody come.** And a woman of about thirty was in the third chair for the whole evening and is not in the photograph and has never been asked why she comes, and about four people in that room wanted to and did not.

## What Movement III of Volume 05 did not do, and what it leaves for Movement IV

**It did not teach, demonstrate, assess or assist anything; nobody was shown anything; and the condition is quoted in none of the ten chapters and paraphrased in none of them. It did not stage the relay, ask the woman who carried it, or name her. It put no second panel and no `* * *` marker in the volume. It did not put a name over the door, and it put no title, office, post, committee, minute, notice, report, form, registration or constitution on anybody, and the only documents this movement produced are a company's pack, about nine hundred leaflets, a prospectus, a piece of hardboard in a corridor, a letter on a clinic board, and two new lines on a wall that is not screwed to anything. It did not open the Exchange book anywhere but Chapter 222, and the book is at forty-five lines and did not open at its eighth sitting, and nobody signed anything in any chapter but the woman of sixty-four on a telephone, which is not a signature. It did not resolve the standing problem, invented no rule about who may be asked, and attached no number to one. It did not get to nine keys, and two exist. It did not empty the box in pencil and did not touch it. It did not let the master leave the fourth pocket of the tool roll and nobody decided anything about its fifth line. It did not name the licensor, name the person who inserted the page, name the clause behind the public version, put Iona or Sorn in any file, or let any message arrive from anybody. It did not put Rafi Pell in a room, name Steve Akers, find the woman of thirty-four with a hip, or say Asha Reed's retired sentence. It did not give Oren Vey a line, and it did not let the man of twenty-two be the person who saw the photograph. It did not settle anything: a company withdrew a print run and a course, a room decided in four minutes not to write a letter, a company answered a correct question correctly, a strike went on, a sister telephoned a plate, and none of it changed a thing anybody can put back. And it did not resolve the relationship: there is no scene, no declaration and no kiss, the two of them are in the same building in one chapter and do not speak, and he is addressed in Chapter 224 by a woman of nineteen and not by anybody else.**

---

A WRITER MUST NOT RE-DERIVE ANY OF THESE. The ten closing headings are ten different headings, which is the first time in this manuscript that ten entries in a row have varied them.**

**A SECOND, REVIEW-DERIVED REPAIR PASS THEN FOUND TEN MORE, EIGHT OF THEM UNREPRODUCIBLE FIGURES IN THE DOCUMENTS AROUND THE BATCH AND TWO OF THEM IN THE CHAPTERS. Both chapter edits are made and neither moved the story.** **(1) CHAPTER 215'S PROSE SAID THE MARKET WAS *TEN DAYS AGO* WHERE ITS OWN LOAD BOOK, IN THE SAME FILE, SAID *NINE DAYS BEFORE*.** The market is the Wednesday of week 70 and Chapter 215 is the Friday of week 71, which is nine days, and the prose now says nine. **The rule this establishes, and it is a new one for this repository: an entry that agrees with its chapter is not evidence that the chapter is right, and recomputing an interval means reading it in both places it appears.** **(2) CHAPTER 220'S BENCH PARAGRAPH OPENED WITH THE SAME NINETY-FOUR CHARACTERS AS CHAPTER 215'S** — *He got back to Lattice Ward at about ten. The lamp was on, the shutter was up, the docket had* — and the two diverged after that point, so a claim of nil duplicated paragraphs at a *full-character* comparison held while the same claim at an *eighty-character prefix* was false. Chapter 220's paragraph is reworded and opens on the lamp still being on when he arrives, and **the four bench paragraphs in this batch — Chapters 215, 216, 219 and 220 — are now four different paragraphs, which is the floor the batch prompt set.** **(3) EIGHT DOCUMENT DEFECTS: a headline hedge figure of 387 that no rule in this repository produces, because the article in the printed hedge rule is optional and the strict and chain branches are one matcher — 401 at 12.57 is the figure; a matcher-provenance claim that one published figure in six reproduces when four of six do and two are out by twenty-five and fourteen; a backward eighty-character sweep that had walked a remembered subset and printed 20 runs, a largest of 94 and a mean of 82.8 where the full 210-chapter index gives 105, 149 and 94.0; a within-batch count of 75 pairs where ten chapters have 45; and a false nil-duplicate claim.** **THE BATCH'S FIGURES, ALL RE-MEASURED ON THE FILES AS THEY NOW STAND: 31,905 words, 18,455 prose and 13,450 load book, 560 raw *about* at 17.55, 401 hedges at 12.57, 238 bold spans at 7.46 with a worst chapter of 9.54, load-book overlap 78, 80, 32, 53, 82, 32, 42, 94, 78, 105 for a mean of 67.6, backward sweep 105 runs / largest 149 / mean 94.0, within-batch 45 of 45 unordered pairs in 149 runs / largest 135 / mean 94.9.
